# fct engine audit — end-to-end transition correctness (2026-07-24)

## What this corrects
Node-boundary PARITY (fct/parity) VERIFIED 53 isolated FCP functions (colour transfers, blur
kernels) bit-for-bit. That is NECESSARY but NOT SUFFICIENT: it does not prove the whole
transitions render correctly. This audit measures the ACTUAL 65 shipped transitions,
engine-vs-headless-FCP, per frame.

## Honest headline
- 65 transitions, mean 17.83 dB, median 16.60 dB engine-vs-headless.
- Only 6/65 are "ok" (>25 dB). 36/65 are bad or broken (<18 dB).
- The node-boundary VERIFIED colour/blur math is fine; the FAILURES are in
  RIG / MOVEMENT / 3D-TRANSFORM / COMPOSITING / OUTPUT-GEOMETRY — the parts the parity
  harness never exercised.

## Method
Per transition, scored engine-vs-headless PSNR at frame 0 (rest=A), mid, last (rest=B).
The endpoint-vs-midpoint signature classifies the root cause:
- endpoints HIGH, mid LOW  -> MOTION bug (rest states right, the animation between is wrong)
- endpoints LOW            -> STRUCTURAL bug (wrong geometry/layer/composite even at rest)
- output dims != 1920x1080 -> DIM/aspect bug
- all moderate             -> mid/resample (kernel ok, resampling/settle ceiling)

## Buckets
| class | n | mean dB | meaning |
|-------|---|---------|---------|
| STRUCTURAL | 31 | 14.4 | wrong geometry/layer/composite/rig even at endpoints — TOP PRIORITY |
| MOTION | 13 | 19.1 | endpoints correct, the movement/timing between is wrong |
| mid/resample | 15 | 22.2 | kernel correct, mid-transition softening/settle ceiling |
| ENDPOINT | 5 | 22.8 | one endpoint (A or B) wrong — layer visibility/timing at rest |
| DIM/aspect | 1 | 15.4 | engine renders wrong canvas size |

## The minimize tool IS the right instrument
fct/minimize.py (ddmin delta-debugger) reduces a broken .motr to the MINIMAL subtree that
still makes engine diverge from REAL FCP-headless, giving the exact node to fix. Already done
for 12 cases (e.g. Stylized__Lower 153->3 nodes, Video_Wall 46->4, Concentric 140->11).
CAVEAT: some minimize runs ABORT at 99 dB headless (Smear, Squares) — meaning the discrepancy
is engine-vs-GUI (colour management), NOT engine-vs-FCP; those are a separate class.

## IN-DEPTH DIAGNOSIS: Wipes__Diagonal (worst minimized case, worst frame 2.60 dB) — 2026-07-24
Minimized 6-node repro: "Transition Diagonal" layer = {"Gradient and background" group (a
light-gray gradient-fill shape "Background") + "Animated mask" (feathered closed bezier, Feather=300,
Aspect 1.5)} composited over "Transition Drop Zones" (image A). Emitters + Transition B stripped.

DECODED FCP behavior (headless, per-corner sampling):
- f0-4: image A held (mean 97).
- f5-14: a light-gray gradient WASHES IN diagonally from TOP-LEFT, reaching full-frame uniform ~195.
- f16-18: a SECOND, DARK wavefront sweeps in from TOP-LEFT over the gray (TL 195→114→0 while BR still 195).
- f20-23: fully BLACK (all corners 0).
=> FCP "Diagonal" is a MOVING REVEAL BAND (two diagonal wavefronts): a leading edge reveals the gray
   gradient over A, a trailing edge conceals it to black. NOT a monotonic write-on envelope.

ENGINE behavior (decoded via FCT_WON_DEBUG write-on coverage trace):
- The engine models the "Animated mask" as a MONOTONIC WRITE-ON envelope (union of the feathered
  quad rasterized at K=12 sub-times over [0,curveTime], per-pixel max). Coverage caps at 85.6%
  (a central vertical band; sepia A remains on L/R edges) and HOLDS — it never reaches 100% and
  never sweeps to black. So engine mean freezes at ~175 while FCP goes 195→0.
TWO ROOT DEFECTS:
  (1) COVERAGE CAP: the finite feathered quad (~1000px + 300 feather) swept along the diagonal
      Position path [(-1046,-2129)@0.30s → (+2105,+1871)@1.07s] leaves the opposite corners
      uncovered; the write-on union tops out at 85.6%. FCP covers the whole frame.
  (2) WRONG MODEL: FCP is a moving band (reveal-then-conceal to black), not monotonic write-on.
      The engine's monotonic-max hold can never reproduce the black exit sweep.
NEXT: replace/augment the write-on model for this mask with the true moving-band reveal (leading
edge reveals gradient, trailing edge conceals) so it (a) reaches full coverage and (b) exits to
black. Verify the Position sweep path + quad size reproduce both wavefront timings. This is a
compositor-model fix (engine/src/compositor + evaluator write-on), not a constant tweak.

### UPDATE 2026-07-24 (aspect-ratio fix landed, commit re(shapes) 16.0→17.26):
DECODED + FIXED defect (1a): the mask shape was rasterizing 1.5× TOO NARROW because the engine
ignored Motion's `<aspectRatio>` ELEMENT (direct child of the shape/mask scenenode). FCP applies it
as a pure shape-LOCAL X-stretch BEFORE the node rotation. Clean-square headless probe: 200-unit
square @ aspect 1.5 = 301×203 (FCP) vs 199×199 (old engine); rot45 → 355×355 vs 283×283 — exactly
the long-hunted 1.2544× gap. NOT the id=153 param (inert). Now parsed (Shape.aspectRatio) + applied
in rasterizeShape.toPixel. Wipes__Diagonal 16.0→17.26 dB, worst frame still 1.14; golden 2123/0.
REMAINING on this slug: (1b) the write-on coverage still doesn't reach full frame, (2) the moving-band
reveal-then-conceal-to-black model, and a SEPARATE gradient-fill bug isolated in the maskgeom static
repro (Background gradient shape renders ~221 vs FCP ~192.5 + feather rings — distinct from geometry).

## Replicator-Clones__Video_Wall_rep (2026-07-24, DIAGNOSED — next target, deep replicator/framing subsystem)
Baseline 9.39 dB (worst frame 19). Signal: FCP is near-BLACK at f0 ([0,0,0]) and dark-gray
[24-32] mid; the ENGINE is much brighter + blue-tinted ([86,99,135] f0, [47,53,63] mid).
Instrumented at t=0: several replicators have in=0 (Replicator Pin 1 in=0 out=1.101; Replicator
Pin 2 in=0 out=0.367; copy 3 in=0 out=0.534) so the engine renders them at f0 — but FCP shows
BLACK. No Sequence-Replicator/birth behavior in the .motr (only a factory-3 "Frame framer"
Framing behavior on the Camera). So FCP's f0-black is NOT a birth-rate stagger; it is the
FRAMING CAMERA pose at t=0 placing the wall OUT OF VIEW (or the framer's own timing out=0 hiding
the framed content), while the engine projects the wall on-screen too early + too bright. ROOT
CAUSE = framing-camera-projected replicator wall visibility/pose at scene start (deep
perspective+replicator subsystem, ~23 dB deficit). Multi-tick: decode the Frame framer camera
animation (Camera Position anim + factory-3 Framing behavior) and the framer scenenode out=0
gating; verify the wall is off-view at f0 and enters as the camera dollies. NOT a quick fix.

## Replicator-Clones__Clone_Spin (2026-07-24 DIAGNOSED — framing-camera subsystem, next)
Baseline 12.6 dB, worst frame 22. f0 matches (25.4, both full warm A). By f21 REAL FCP shows a
SMALL bluish B rectangle centre-left on a mostly-BLACK frame (the content has spun+dollied AWAY,
shrinking to a distant framed card), while the ENGINE shows FULL-FRAME warm A (autumn lake) with a
small spinning B tile on top — i.e. the engine never dollies/spins the A content out; it holds A
static full-frame. Structure: group "Comp" {Transition B in=0.033} + layer "Drop Zones" {Transition
A out=1.868} under a Camera with a factory-1 "Framing" behavior (Framing=4, Offset Path Apex 0.29,
Framing Offset X=1450/Y=980/Z=5332, Transition=1). The "spin" is the FRAMING CAMERA orbiting +
dollying the framed target over the transition; the engine's framedCameraBasis/projectFramed pose
is NOT animating the orbit/zoom, so A stays full-frame and bright at late t. ROOT CAUSE = framing-
camera pose animation over time (shared perspective subsystem, ~27.7 deficit) — same class as
Video_Wall_rep f0 (wall shown too early) and Perspective__3D_Rectangle_cam. Multi-tick: decode the
factory-1 Framing behavior's per-frame camera pose (position/orientation/AOV cross-blend across
Position/Rotation Transition Time, Offset Path Apex arc) and drive projectFramed with it. NOT a
quick fix.

## FRAMING-CAMERA subsystem — deep diagnosis (2026-07-24, Clone_Spin lead case)
The framing camera (factory-1/3 "Framing" behavior) is the shared root cause of the 3 remaining
low framed cases: Clone_Spin (12.6), Video_Wall_rep f0 residual (13.4), Perspective (13.68).
Decoded FCP motion for Clone_Spin (headless bbox track): A fills frame f0 → shrinks+moves LEFT
(cx 959→48) to a distant point by f8 → then B GROWS back in (cx 48→606, colour warm→cool) f10-22.
It is a continuous camera dolly+orbit through the transition.

ENGINE behavior (measured): f0 full A (ok); f3-6 shrinks but drifts RIGHT (cx 789-804, FCP goes
LEFT); f9-18 FROZEN at a fixed small tile (cx=933 w=572 identical every frame); f21 SNAPS back to
FULL-FRAME WARM A [127,87,65] while FCP shows small dark B [14,16,20].

ROOT CAUSES (multiple, in engine/src/evaluator/framing.ts resolveFramedWallPose single-framer branch
`if (framing.length===1)`, lines ~372-406):
1. HOLD-AFTER-APEX: the code dollies keyNearA→far(=framePose B) over [0, tFarC=apex*endC≈0.58s] then
   returns `far` STATICALLY for all t>tFarC → the frozen f9-18. FCP does NOT hold; it continues the
   dolly (B grows back toward the viewer). Needs a THIRD keyframe (B framed near/growing) after apex,
   or a continuous computeFraming(t) sample, not a 2-key hold.
2. f21 FULL-A: at t=1.723 the wall-pose DOES return the small oblique `far` pose (verified: farDist
   2212, farPos[-256,3142,-1067]), and BOTH Transition A & B are framed=true/forceA=false — yet the
   render shows full-frame warm A. So the framing PROJECTION maps the A tile (world 0,0,0) to fill the
   frame under the far pose (projectFramed center-translate + uniform ps-scale can't recede a
   full-frame plane to a point the way true perspective quad-corner projection does). The affine
   center-scale framing model is the deeper limitation.
3. LEFT-vs-RIGHT drift (f3-6): the dolly direction/orbit is mirrored/off vs FCP.

NEXT (multi-tick, RULE 2): rework the single-framer to (a) sample computeFraming continuously over
[in,out] with the Ease Out Curve (id 213) instead of the 2-key near→far→HOLD, and (b) project the
framed drop-zone as a true perspective QUAD (4 corners through projectFramed) so a receding plane
shrinks to a point/trapezoid, not a center-scaled full-frame blit. NOTE: the cron heartbeat reverts
uncommitted working-tree debug between ticks — commit a scratch debug branch or keep instrumentation
minimal + within one tick. My resolveFramedPose single-framer animation attempt did NOT fire because
resolveFramedWallPose intercepts framing.length===1 first — edit the WALL-pose branch, not
resolveFramedPose.

## FRAMING-CAMERA — two hypotheses DISPROVEN by measurement (2026-07-24)
Continuing the framing-camera subsystem (Clone_Spin/Video_Wall_rep/Perspective). Two decoded-looking
fixes were IMPLEMENTED + MEASURED + REVERTED because they regressed the target:

NEGATIVE RESULT 1 — resolveFramedPose single-framer animation: added a start→framePose ease-out dolly
for always-on framers. Did NOT fire — resolveFramedWallPose intercepts framing.length===1 FIRST and
returns before resolveFramedPose is reached. (Edit resolveFramedWallPose, not resolveFramedPose.)

NEGATIVE RESULT 2 — PERSPECTIVE-QUAD projection of framed drop zones (project the tile's 4 world
corners through projectFramed + renderPerspectiveQuad, replacing the affine centre-translate+
uniform-scale). Hypothesis: a receding plane should shrink to a point. MEASURED across all framed
cases: Clone_Spin 12.6→11.73, Video_Wall 84.09→83.84, Concentric 40.08→39.99, Perspective
13.68→13.43, Video_Wall_rep 13.39→13.37. EVERYTHING slightly WORSE, including the target. So the
affine centre-scale framing is CLOSER to FCP than a true perspective quad — the framed drop zones are
NOT meant to recede as perspective quads in this projection. REVERTED. Do NOT retry perspective-quad.

CONCLUSION: the Clone_Spin f21 divergence (engine full-frame warm A vs FCP small dark B) is NOT a
projection-geometry problem. It is the POSE ANIMATION (resolveFramedWallPose holds `far` statically
after apex instead of continuing the dolly) AND/OR the RETIME / which-drop-zone-shows-when logic
(the retime-wrap forceSourceA re-showing A at the tail). Next angle: instrument which layer paints at
f21 and whether forceSourceA fires; and whether FCP's computeFraming samples the B-target transform
CONTINUOUSLY (the B tile's own animated transform pulling the framing) rather than a fixed far pose.
Still deep/multi-tick; the two cheap geometry hypotheses are now ruled out.

## Stylized/Center — SHAPE CROP fix (2026-07-24, commit 2c3080f) + remaining tail-timing
FIXED a real, general engine gap: shapes ignored their Transform>Crop entirely.
 - Root cause 1: rasterizeShape never received/applied Left/Right/Top/Bottom crop.
 - Root cause 2: evaluator resolved crop at raw scene timeSec, but Crop is a Transform-block
   param authored in the SAME local frame as Position/Scale. Center's "Right full" panel keys its
   Crop in NEGATIVE local time [-1.0..-0.7s]; at positive timeSec it clamped to the last keyframe
   (crop=0, uncropped) so the white panel never shrank and covered Transition B all clip.
 - Fix: (1) evaluator resolves crop at curveTime (offset-re-anchored, byte-neutral where
   curveTime==timeSec); (2) rasterizeShape takes crop, clips alpha to the cropped local rect
   (4 corners → toPixel → AA-fill → multiply). Wired into fillColor + solidPanel shape paths.
 - Result: Center minimized f0 6.5->99 dB; full-scene 11.18->12.3 dB. Golden 2123/0, Lower unchanged.
REMAINING (deeper, deferred): full-scene f16-f20 the engine holds the bright white-panel peak ~4
frames too long (FCP decays white->cool B by f16; engine not until f20+). This is the panel-retime
curveTime RATE for Center (rate≈0.5) making the crop/opacity EXIT sweep too slow in the tail, PLUS
the layer-level inverted Image Mask (Mask Source 554019971 "Shapes for image mask", Invert=1) that
reveals B — the minimized case is DEGENERATE here (minimize stripped the mask source node, so the
reveal can't reproduce; min stalls ~10.35 dB). Next: decode Center's panel-retime exit rate and the
inverted-Image-Mask B-reveal compositing (needs the mask source present — work against the FULL scene,
not the minimized repro). Shape Crop capability itself is DONE and correct.

## Stylized/Center — shape Crop FIXED + remaining exit-timing (2026-07-24, commit 2c3080f)
FIXED a real, general engine gap: shapes ignored their Transform>Crop entirely.
 - rasterizeShape now takes an optional crop and clips the shape alpha to the cropped
   local rect (4 corners through the same toPixel as the fill, AA-filled, multiplied in).
   Wired into BOTH the fillColor and solidPanel shape paths.
 - evaluator resolves crop at the layer-local `curveTime` (offset-re-anchored), NOT raw
   scene timeSec. Center's "Right full" panel keys its Crop in NEGATIVE local time
   [-1.0s..-0.7s]; at raw timeSec they clamped to the last keyframe (crop=0, uncropped)
   so the white panel never shrank and covered Transition B all clip. curveTime lands the
   crop sweep in the visible window. Byte-neutral for non-re-anchored layers (timeSec==curveTime).
 - Center minimized f0 6.5->99 dB (panel now correctly cropped to nothing early = FCP black).
   Golden 2123/0. Stylized/Lower + other panels unchanged (they carry no crop).
REMAINING (full-scene Center 11.18->12.3 dB, NOT yet fixed): the white panels/flash reach
   their bright peak ~f12 in BOTH engine and FCP, but FCP DECAYS to cool B by f16
   ([142,154,177]->[103,118,149]) while the engine HOLDS the bright wash until ~f20 (f16
   engine [234,241,243] vs FCP [142,154,177], 7.2 dB). The crop/panel EXIT timing is ~4
   frames too slow. Root: the isSolidPanel panel-retime curveTime rate (=(out-in)/endSec,
   ≈0.5 for Center) opens the crop too slowly in the tail. The minimized case is DEGENERATE
   for this (its Image Mask source 554019971 = "Shapes for image mask" group was stripped by
   minimize, so the inverted-mask B-reveal can't reproduce there — min stalls at 10.35).
   NEXT: decode the exact panel media->clip time map for Center (the +in-anchored rate vs the
   late-window out/endSec model) so the crop CLEARS on FCP's schedule; or model the layer-level
   inverted Image Mask (Mask Source=554019971, Invert=1) reveal against the full scene.

## Stylized/Center — ROOT CAUSE of the remaining tail-timing: animationEndSec inflated 2× (2026-07-24)
The full-scene crop CLOSE fires ~2 frames late (f18 vs FCP f16) purely because
`animationEndSec` is INFLATED: full scene = 4.671s, but the true visual end ≈ 2.269s
(exactly what the MINIMIZED case computes once the culprit is stripped).
CULPRIT: node "Final Bottom Mask" (scenenode factoryID=14, id 642749) — a shape INSIDE the
"Shapes for image mask" group (id 554019971, the inverted Image-Mask SOURCE). It has
timing in=3.871s / out=5.272s and Position-X + Scale-X keyframes out to 4.638s / 4.671s.
Those keys win the maxT walk (parser/index.ts ~L1240) because sceneDurSec=5.3s (the padded
159f/30fps scene duration) does NOT cap them (4.67 < 5.3), and the node is not loop-exempt
yet still counted. Its `in=3.871s` is PAST the true ~2.3s transition — it's a trailing
mask-geometry drift, NOT visible-transition content. GT settle window confirms Center
settles ~frame idx 36 (t0_idx 12), consistent with ~2.3s not 4.67s.
WITH endSec≈2.269: the isSolidPanel panel-retime rate=(out-in)/endSec would put the Bottom
crop close at f16.7 — MATCHING FCP's f16 decay. So fixing the endSec inflation fixes the
whole tail-timing divergence (est. full-scene Center 12.3 -> ~16+ dB).
PROPOSED FIX (NOT yet applied — HIGH REGRESSION RISK on the shared animationEndSec walk with
the 65-gate disabled): exclude transform curves whose OWNING node's timeline `in` sits past a
large fraction of the visible span (or: exclude curves under a node that lives inside an
Image-Mask SOURCE group — mask-geometry, not rendered content). Both need the full 65-gate to
verify no regression across the other Kinetic/Stylized panels. Deferred until the gate can run.
The shape-Crop capability itself (commit 2c3080f) is DONE, correct, and independently shipped.

The full scene's animationEndSec = 4.671s but the TRUE transition end is ~2.269s (the minimized

The inflation source: node "Final Bottom Mask" (id 642749, factoryID=14) — a shape INSIDE the group
"Shapes for image mask" (id 554019971 = the Comp's inverted Image-Mask SOURCE). It has timing
in=3.871s / out=5.272s and Position-X / Scale-X keyframes at 4.57-4.671s. Those late keys win the
maxT walk in parser/index.ts. The sceneDur cap (Math.min(sec, sceneDurSec)) does NOT clamp them
because sceneDurSec = <duration>159</duration>/30 = 5.3s (the PADDED scene duration), and 4.671 < 5.3.
CONSEQUENCE: with endSec=4.671 the isSolidPanel panel-retime rate = (out-in)/endSec = 5.272/4.671 =
1.13, so the crop-Bottom EXIT (local [2.102,2.269]s, closes the white panel) fires at frame ~18
instead of frame ~16 => the engine holds the bright wash ~2-4 frames too long (f16 engine
[234,241,243] vs FCP [142,154,177]). Verified by hand: with the correct endSec≈2.269 the crop-Bottom
close lands at f16, matching FCP.
WHY NOT FIXED YET: the fix is to stop "Final Bottom Mask"'s late keys from inflating animationEndSec.
"Final Bottom Mask" is a MASK-SOURCE shape (in="3.871s" is PAST the true ~2.3s visual end; the node
doesn't even become active until then). But animationEndSec's maxT walk is a DEEPLY shared, heavily-
tuned function (Close_and_Open / Up-Over / Flash / Light Sweep / Bloom / Color Planes all have bespoke
exclusions), the full 65-gate is DISABLED, and a speculative "exclude nodes whose in > k*sceneDur"
rule can't be regression-verified here. Candidate decode-derived rule to try WITH a gate: exclude
curves whose enclosing scenenode's timeline `in` >= playRange/transition true-end (or whose nearest
group is an image-mask-source group AND in > 0.6*sceneDur). Needs the full gate to land safely.

## Stylized/Center — DISPROVEN: forcing endSec=2.269 on the full scene (2026-07-24)
Tested the "animationEndSec inflated 2×" hypothesis by env-forcing the full-scene endSec to the
minimized value (2.269s) and re-rendering all 24 frames. Result: REGRESSED 12.3 -> 9.71 dB. The
peak shifted to f15-18 (too late) and the decay never lands in-window. So the full scene GENUINELY
needs the longer ~4.67s window for its OTHER panels/Rig — the minimized case's 2.269s is correct only
for ITS stripped subset. The tail-timing divergence is NOT a simple global endSec error; the panels'
per-node retime (curveTime rate) is the real lever and differs per panel. Do NOT globally shorten
Center's endSec. The shape-Crop capability (commit 2c3080f) remains the correct, shipped win; the
Center tail-timing needs per-panel retime decode against the full scene, not a global endSec change.

## Movements/Drop_In (12.03) — ROOT CAUSE: particle-emitter sim (T-B2) unbuilt (2026-07-24)
Diagnosed to root cause. Drop_In's "Drop Impact" is a PARTICLE SYSTEM: 2 Emitters (factoryID=19)
+ 3 Particle Cells + 3 Gravity behaviors. The emitters' Particle Source = the Blur nodes that
reference Transition B; particles carry B content and are dropped in under GRAVITY (the bounce).
FCP renders B as small gravity-driven particle(s) over a warm-A background:
  FCP warm_A_frac f2/f4/f8 = 0.97/0.70/0.73, cool_B_frac = 0.03/0.24/0.14 (B is a SMALL bouncing
  element; A shows through). ENGINE = cool_B 0.75/0.75/0.60, warm_A ~0.01-0.14 (B covers the frame).
The engine has NO particle simulation: the parser lifts EmitterParams/ParticleCellParams (T-B1) but
"no evaluator/compositor path yet reads these — T-B2 will run the particle sim off this schema".
So the engine renders the B particle-cell prototype as a full-frame drop zone (wtY per the bounce
curve, but a full-frame card at wtY±345 still covers everything => no A shows). This is why B floods
the frame from f2. The authored B Position-Y curve (719,-416,282,-99,89,-41,0) CANNOT by itself
produce FCP's positions (B 97% off-screen at f2 needs wtY≈-1050, curve max is -416) — because the
real motion is the PARTICLE + GRAVITY sim, not the cell's own transform.
=> Drop_In needs the T-B2 particle simulation subsystem (large, unbuilt). Correctly diagnosed deep
   blocker, NOT a contained transform bug. Negating B Position-Y (tested) does NOT help (phase wrong,
   amplitude still tiny). endSec/retime does not explain it either.
NOTE: the MINIMIZED Drop_In repro is a CORRUPTED oracle — its headless frames are MIXED RESOLUTION
(f0-f21 = 1280x720, f22-23 = 1920x1080), an artifact of minimizing a particle scene. The FULL scene
(consistently 1920x1080) is the authoritative oracle. Do not trust the minimized Drop_In geometry.

## Movements/Drop_In — particle sim (T-B2) blocked on Motion particle PRNG (2026-07-24, cont.)
Deeper dive on the emitter/cell schema (T-B1 already lifts it fully). Drop_In's "Drop Impact"
= 2 emitters, 3 cells, each firing initialNumber=379 B-content sprite particles at speed
2409±1228 units/s, life ~0.7-0.8s, gravity accel=30, scale 0.37, emissionAngle π range 4.2rad
— a one-shot SPLASH BURST of ~1137 fast B-image shards on impact, over a warm-A background.
The particle SOURCE is the B image (Blur11/Blur11 copy → drop zone B), so the shards carry B
content; that's why FCP shows scattered cool-B fragments over warm A while the shards fly + fall.
BLOCKER (same class as Noise/Static host-RNG): each particle's spawn position/velocity/scale is
drawn from Motion's proprietary particle PRNG seeded by emitterSeed=25785 + cell randomSeed=18155
and the *Randomness fields (speedRandomness 1228, lifeRandomness 8, scaleRandomness 0.476). At
f2-f8 the ~1137 shards are mid-flight and SPARSE, so their EXACT positions (not just statistics)
determine each pixel. A stochastic sim with a different PRNG yields a statistically-similar but
pixel-different splash → won't raise engine-vs-FCP PSNR against a specific frame. Matching Drop_In
requires reverse-engineering Motion's particle RNG from the binary (Filters.bundle / the particle
kernel) — the exact same recovery problem as BadTV Static / Noise. Building the advect/gravity/
render loop (T-B2) is straightforward off the T-B1 schema; the RNG is the true blocker.
STATUS: correctly root-caused; deferred pending particle-PRNG binary RE (shared with Noise/Static).
The 4 particle transitions (Drop_In 12.03, Wipes/Stylized Diagonal 16.6, Glide 18.31) all share it.
Confirmed NOT fixable by transform/endSec: forcing endSec {1.468,2.05,2.5} left warm-A frac ~0.01-0.27
vs FCP 0.5-0.97 (B floods regardless of bounce timing); negating B Position-Y (tested) also fails.

## Movements/Drop_In — REFINED full-scene mechanism (2026-07-24, cont.2)
Traced the FULL-scene (reliable oracle) mechanism precisely. warm_A_frac trajectory:
  f0-1: 1.00 (all A) → gradual erosion to ~0.50 by f12-f21 → SNAP to 0.00 at f22 (B card covers).
So the mechanism is THREE interacting parts, none of which the engine renders correctly:
 1. Transition A (static full-frame, in=0/out=endSec) is VISIBLE as the top/background for the whole
    transition — i.e. A is effectively ON TOP of the B card until the very end. Engine renders the B
    card on top (first-declared-on-top) so B floods from f2. NOTE the z-order here looks LAST-declared-
    on-top (A declared after B, A stays visible) — OPPOSITE of Switch's first-declared-on-top. This is
    a real z-order conflict to resolve (drop-zone A/B ordering may follow a different rule than shapes).
 2. The B CONTENT during f2-f21 is the PARTICLE SPLASH (emitters emit ~1137 B-image shards under
    gravity), gradually accumulating over A (1.0→0.5 erosion). Engine's emitter-sim (emitter-sim.ts,
    T-B2 SHIPPED) uses a PLACEHOLDER hash01 RNG (line ~93), not Motion's particle PRNG seeded by
    emitterSeed 25785 / cell randomSeed 18155 — so shard positions are pixel-wrong (the documented
    shared blocker w/ Noise/Static).
 3. The "Transition B" full-frame CARD settles to center (Y curve → 0 at t=1.268s) and takes over the
    frame at f22. Its Position-Y curve (719→-416→282→…→0) crosses ZERO at t≈0.127s so the engine
    correctly evaluates B centered at f2 — but that card should be BEHIND A (hidden) until it settles.
KEY CORRECTION to earlier notes: the engine DOES evaluate B's Y curve correctly (B is genuinely
centered at f2 per the curve); the bug is NOT the curve/endSec/sign. The bug is (a) z-order (B card
should be behind A / not cover A mid-transition) and (b) the particle splash RNG.
T-B2 particle sim IS shipped (emitter-sim.ts, applyEmitterSim wired at index.ts ~L1339) but uses a
non-Motion RNG. So the real remaining work: (1) decode the drop-zone A/B z-order rule for Drop_In,
(2) recover Motion's particle PRNG (shared w/ Noise/Static binary RE). Both deep; Drop_In needs both.

## Movements/Drop_In — z-order default is the DOMINANT bug (2026-07-24, cont.3)
Pinned the dominant visual error (B floods, A hidden) to the LAYER PAINT ORDER:
 - "Drop Impact" contains [Blur11copy, Blur11, Group(emitters), Transition B, Transition A] and has
   NO clone layer, so isFlatCoplanarStack() returns FALSE (it requires >=1 clone). The group therefore
   uses the DEFAULT paint order `visibleChildren[len-1-idx]` = reverse-DOM = last-declared painted
   FIRST (bottom) / first-declared on TOP. => A (last-declared) is at the BOTTOM, B on TOP → B covers A.
 - FCP shows A on top (warm) for f0-f21, B only takes over at f22. So Drop_In wants A (last-declared)
   on TOP = FORWARD DOM order (first-declared bottom) — the OPPOSITE of the default reverse-DOM.
CONFLICT: Switch (flat-stack, has clones) needs first-declared-on-top; Drop_In (no clones, default
path) needs first-declared-BOTTOM. The default reverse-DOM order is wrong for the Drop_In A/B pair.
This is a z-order rule that needs the full 65-gate to change safely (the default order governs MANY
non-flat-stack groups) — DISABLED gate blocks verification. NOT a clean single-tick fix.
Even with A-on-top fixed, part 2 (particle splash accumulating B over A, f2-f21 erosion 1.0→0.5) still
needs Motion's particle PRNG. So Drop_In = z-order (gate-blocked) + particle PRNG (binary RE). Deferred.
Confirmed the engine evaluates B's Y curve CORRECTLY (B centered at f2 because the 719→-416 curve
crosses zero at t≈0.127s); earlier "curve/endSec/sign" hypotheses are all WRONG and closed.

## Movements/Drop_In — z-order fix VERIFIED (forward-DOM / A-on-top), gate-blocked to generalize (2026-07-24)
Empirically CONFIRMED the z-order fix: forcing the non-flat-stack default paint order to FORWARD-DOM
(first-declared bottom, last-declared TOP) for Drop_In makes A paint on top → warm_A f2/f4/f8 = 
1.00/0.96/0.91 (was ~0.01), MATCHING FCP's A-dominant direction (0.97/0.70/0.73). So the dominant
"B floods the frame" error IS the paint order.
BUT the rule can't be globally flipped: Push/Scale/Fall are A-FIRST in DOM and currently correct with
reverse-DOM (A already ends up on top). Drop_In is B-FIRST so reverse-DOM wrongly puts B on top.
The CONSISTENT rule for all of them is "**Transition A painted ON TOP of Transition B**" (A=source/from
stays on top and animates/erodes away to reveal B) — this holds for Push (A-first, A on top) AND
Drop_In (B-first, needs A on top). Implementing "A-on-top" (order Transition-A source last) is a clean
structural rule keyed on source.type transitionA/transitionB — NOT a slug name. HOWEVER it is a
cross-cutting z-order change that governs every non-flat-stack A/B group, so it MUST be verified on the
full 65-gate before shipping (a transition that legitimately reveals B ON TOP — e.g. a B-wipe-in — would
regress). The 65-gate is DISABLED per the per-node-loop policy, so this fix is correctly PARKED pending
a gate run. Residual after z-order: FCP's A erodes to ~0.5 mid-transition via the B-particle splash
(needs Motion particle PRNG) — so Drop_In also needs the shared PRNG work. Both blockers documented.

## Movements/Drop_In — FINAL: z-order + particles are COUPLED, neither helps alone (2026-07-24)
Scored the A-on-top (forward-DOM) render vs FCP: 11.16 dB — WORSE than the current 12.03. Reason:
without the B-particle erosion (which needs Motion's PRNG), A-on-top shows PURE A (warm_A ~1.0),
which is FURTHER from FCP's A+B-particle MIX (~0.5-0.7) in aggregate MSE than the current B-flood.
So the z-order fix and the particle sim are COUPLED — you must fix BOTH together to raise PSNR;
fixing z-order alone regresses, and fixing particles alone can't (B card still floods on top).
Also: A-on-top can't be globally applied anyway (B-first non-clone slugs Duplicate 21.56, Light_Noise
17.43, Leaves 16.52, Veil 16.27 currently work with B-on-top). The correct scoping is "group contains
an emitter/particle system => A-on-top + particle reveal", but that is ONLY correct once particles
render (PRNG). CONCLUSION: Drop_In is a single coupled blocker = {A/B z-order tied to particle reveal}
+ {Motion particle PRNG, shared w/ Noise/Static}. Cannot be improved incrementally; needs the particle
PRNG binary RE first, then the coupled z-order falls out. Fully diagnosed; parked on PRNG. Golden 2123/0.

## Video_Wall (min, 84.09) — reveal-ramp timing: measured but needs behavior decode (2026-07-24)
Investigated the tail divergence (f20-23: engine 23% black top vs FCP 2%). Root: the single-delayed-
framer REVEAL path (resolveFramedPose, tin=0.868>0) slides the framed B tile up over its window with a
hand-tuned ramp (REVEAL_HOLD=0.6 hold + linear + HOLD_MUL=2.6 slide distance). MEASURED FCP-headless
content-top trajectory: FULLY off (black) through window-frac 0.71 (f14-19), then top-edge slides
0.66→0.35→0.13→0.02 over frac 0.79/0.87/0.95/1.03 (f20-23) — a flat HOLD then a fast near-linear/ease-out
rush. The current k=0.6 starts the slide ~2 frames too early. BUT raising k to 0.72 REGRESSED the net
(84.09→83.79): it fixed f16-19 (correctly black) yet the HOLD_MUL=2.6 slide distance is then too large to
arrive by the window end, so f20-23 stay too-black. k (hold) and HOLD_MUL (slide distance) are COUPLED,
and no single linear/power/sin ramp reproduces the measured "flat-hold-then-rush" with a fixed HOLD_MUL
(tested u^6/u^8/u^10/sin/shifted-linear — all miss). This is a genuine RULE-2 case: the reveal ramp is an
un-decoded 2-constant approximation; the correct fix is to decode the Framing behavior's temporal model
(Position Transition Time=1 + Ease Out Curve=10 + Offset Path Apex) from the binary, not piecewise-fit
the constants. Measurement saved in the framing.ts comment for that decode. Reverted to 0.6 (no regression
shipped). Golden 2123/0. NOTE: full Video_Wall uses the 2-framer wall path (proxy+content), not this
reveal path — only the min-repro (1 framer) exercises it; the full scene's tail is the wall-pose settle.

## Wipes__Diagonal (min, 18.42, worst 2.41 @ f19-23) — mask reveal-through to B, tail bug (2026-07-24)
The minimized case's tail (f17-23) diverges hard: FCP goes to BLACK ([0,0,0]) while the engine holds
the Background gray [193,192,191]. Diagnosed:
- The Animated mask (finite feathered quad, aspectRatio 1.5, feather 300) sweeps diagonally from
  (-1045,-2129) to (+2105,+1870) over curveTime 0→0.767s. By f19 the quad center is (1569,1190) — WAY
  outside the ~1280×720 frame (bottom-right), i.e. the quad has EXITED.
- FCP behavior (measured): the swept region reveals the layer BEHIND the Background = Transition B.
  FULL-scene oracle confirms: f11-15 green wipe overlay → f19-23 settles on cool B [100,115,147]. In the
  MINIMIZED case B (and the green gradient/particles) are STRIPPED, so "reveal B" = reveal BLACK → the
  tail correctly goes black in FCP.
- ENGINE bug: the write-on monotonic-max envelope KEEPS the Background gray where the mask has swept
  (applyMask keeps group content where mask alpha HIGH). FCP instead REVEALS THROUGH the swept region to
  Transition B behind. So the engine never shows the B-reveal (black in the min-repro) — it holds gray.
- This is NOT the offset-shift (tested FCT_TEST_NOMASKSHIFT: removing it regressed f6 AND left the tail
  gray — the tail bug is independent of the sweep timing). NOT a 1-line polarity flip either: at f11 FCP
  shows the Background/green in the swept region (monotonic reveal), THEN f17-23 the SAME region shows B —
  a two-phase reveal (Background-first, then B-through) that the single monotonic-max matte can't express.
ROOT: the diagonal-wipe REVEAL must composite the mask so the swept region shows the LAYER BEHIND
(Transition B), not hold the masked group's own content. This is the wipe-reveal subsystem (shared with
the green-gradient + emitter paint-stroke path). Contained + real (RULE 1) but needs the reveal-through
compositing, not a constant tweak. Full-scene Diagonal 16.6 dB; the green overlay + B-reveal are the
dominant full-scene terms. Parked with precise root cause; no regression shipped (reverted the probe).

## Wipes__Diagonal — write-on vs instantaneous mask: the REAL model is instantaneous (2026-07-24 cont)
Tested FCT_TEST_NOWRITEON (instantaneous single-transform mask instead of the monotonic-max write-on
envelope): min-case 18.42→18.79, worst 2.41→6.25 — the tail wipe-out is RECOVERED (f19-23 no longer
stuck at bright gray; the moving feathered band's trailing edge uncovers the Background as FCP does).
So the write-on monotonic-max is WRONG for the exit phase — the physically-correct model is the
INSTANTANEOUS feathered band (a moving reveal that un-reveals as it passes). BUT instantaneous made the
FILL phase worse: f11 22.1→13.6 dB (FCP uniform solid gray [194], engine partial [179,160,147]).
ROOT of the f11 regression: the mask's Feather (300) is scaled by the transform magnification (~3.5× at
f11 via the Scale ramp 0.686→5.94) → ~1050px soft edge. With the quad center off-frame at (-50,-866),
the frame sees mostly the FEATHERED gradient (partial) not the solid core, so instantaneous under-fills.
FCP shows solid gray → FCP's feather does NOT blow up to 1050px OR the quad core covers the frame. So the
true fix is the FEATHER SCALING / quad geometry (feather likely in a FIXED/screen unit or clamped, not
local-units × transform-mag), which would make instantaneous correct in BOTH phases and let write-on be
dropped. NET on the min-case, pure-instantaneous is +0.37/worst+3.8 — a real improvement — but it trades
f11 for the tail; not shipping until the feather geometry is decoded so instantaneous is right everywhere
(RULE 2: don't ship the partial trade; fix the feather root cause). Write-on also affects Stylized__
Diagonal (same motr) + Center_Reveal/Light_Sweep/Glide masks (shared mechanism) → needs the gate anyway.
NEXT: decode Motion mask Feather units (fixed screen px vs shape-local × scale) from a headless probe;
if fixed-screen, instantaneous+correct-feather fixes the whole wipe and retires the write-on compromise.

## Wipes__Diagonal — refined: two-phase WIDE feathered band, fixed-feather NOT the fix (2026-07-24)
Tested instantaneous + FIXED feather (feather NOT × transform-mag): min 18.38 (worse than instantaneous-
alone 18.79); f11 still partial [180,162,149] vs FCP solid [194]. So the f11 fill under-coverage is NOT
the feather-unit scaling — it's that the instantaneous quad genuinely doesn't FILL the frame at f11 with
a solid core (the large scaled+feathered quad's in-frame portion is mostly within the soft gradient).
CONFIRMED MODEL: the mask is a WIDE moving feathered band. FCP f11-15 uniform-solid gray = the band's
LEADING edge has fully passed the frame (all revealed) and the TRAILING edge hasn't arrived; f17-23 the
TRAILING edge sweeps through → wipe-out to black. The engine's write-on monotonic-max nails the fill
(f11=22) by union-ing sweep positions but WRONGLY persists into the exit (holds gray, misses the trailing
wipe). Pure-instantaneous nails the exit but under-fills f11 (the single soft quad isn't solid across the
frame). NEITHER single mode is right. The true model needs the band's leading+trailing feathered edges
BOTH tracked instantaneously with the correct quad width so the solid core spans the frame f11-15 then the
trailing edge exits f17-23. This is a mask-band-geometry rebuild (quad extent + feather profile), and it's
a SHARED mechanism (Stylized__Diagonal same motr, + Center_Reveal/Light_Sweep/Glide masks) so it needs the
65-gate. Best min-case lever found: pure-instantaneous +0.37 mean/+3.8 worst, but it trades f11 → not
shipped (RULE 2, partial). Parked with the exact two-phase model + the tried-and-rejected fixed-feather.

## Wipes__Diagonal — COMPLETE decoded model: two-front hysteresis band (2026-07-24)
Corner-sampled FCP proves the exact mechanism (TL/TR/BL/BR over time):
  f0-5:  warm A [140,90,60] (Transition A; mask not yet active)
  f8:    TL=195 BR=103 — LEADING reveal front sweeping TL→BR (gray Background appears)
  f11-15: all ~195 — fully revealed gray PLATEAU (leading front has passed the whole frame)
  f17:   TL=26 BR=195 — TRAILING wipe-out front sweeping TL→BR (gray→black)
  f19-23: →black (trailing front has passed the whole frame)
So it is a HYSTERESIS BAND with TWO fronts moving the same direction:
  • LEADING front = MONOTONIC reveal (once a pixel is revealed it stays) — this is the current write-on
    monotonic-max, and it is CORRECT for f6-15 (that's why write-on gives f11=22 dB).
  • TRAILING front = the quad's trailing edge; where it has PASSED, the pixel UN-reveals (→ behind = black
    in the min-repro / B in the full scene). The current write-on has NO trailing front → holds gray forever
    → the f17-23 = 2.4 dB bug.
Geometry (verified): the mask hexagon (local X∈[-556,457]~1013, Y∈[-216,330]~546) has Scale X=2.088 static,
Scale Y ramping 0.686→5.94, translating Pos (-1045,-2129)→(2105,1870) over curveTime 0→0.767. So the quad
is a growing, translating band; its leading edge reveals early, it plateaus over the frame f13-15 (quad
covers full frame), and its trailing (top-left) edge enters the frame ~f17 → wipe-out. Pure-instantaneous
captures both fronts but UNDER-fills f11 (quad still small then); monotonic captures the leading fill but
never the trailing wipe.
CORRECT FIX (decoded, to implement): writeOn reveal(p) = (leadingCovered(p) monotonic) AND NOT
(trailingPassed(p)). trailingPassed = the CURRENT instantaneous quad no longer covers p AND p is on the
already-swept side of the current quad (behind the trailing edge along the sweep direction). Implement in
the evaluator's writeOn envelope: keep the monotonic union for the leading reveal, but intersect with the
CURRENT-frame quad's trailing half-plane (sweep-direction test) so vacated pixels un-reveal. SHARED
mechanism (Stylized__Diagonal same motr + Center_Reveal/Light_Sweep/Glide) → gate-verify. This is the real
root cause; not a constant tweak. Model fully decoded; implementation is the next concrete step.

## Wipes__Diagonal — trailing-edge IMPL attempt + coordinate fix (2026-07-24)
Implemented the two-front trailing-edge cut in the compositor (union leading reveal, then un-reveal
pixels behind the current quad's rear edge along the sweep axis). Two attempts BOTH failed to fire
(18.42→18.46/18.41, worst 2.41 unchanged) — ROOT: the sweep DIRECTION was derived wrong.
- Derived from raw Position delta (world (3150,3999)) → screen (+x,−y) = up-right.
- EMPIRICAL (FCP black-region centroid over f17→f19: (315,317)→(929,525)) → screen (+x,+y) = DOWN-right.
The mismatch is the LIFTED-MASK Y-FLIP (the engine negates positionY for lifted `<mask>` shapes, memory
2026-07-24): the on-screen motion is +y (down), opposite the raw-Position-derived −y. So the trailing-edge
half-plane test MUST use the ACTUAL on-screen sweep direction, which is robustly obtained from the
RASTERIZED mask centroids at two sub-times (they already include the flip/scale/anchor), NOT from the raw
transform translation.
CORRECT IMPL PLAN (next): in the writeOn compositor block, (1) rasterize the quad at the first and last
sub-time, (2) compute each footprint centroid, (3) sweepDir = normalize(lastCentroid − firstCentroid)
[screen space, includes the Y-flip], (4) rear cutoff = min projection of the CURRENT quad's transformed
vertices onto sweepDir (screen coords), (5) un-reveal env pixels with projection < cutoff. Verified fronts:
reveal centroid f8(709,449)→f10(941,533); black-wipe centroid f17(315,317)→f19(929,525) — both +x,+y.
SHARED mechanism (Stylized__Diagonal same motr + Center_Reveal/Light_Sweep/Glide) → gate-verify before
shipping. Baseline restored (18.42, golden 2123/0); no partial change shipped (RULE 2).

## 360°__360°_Push (9.55 full / min 12.33) — headless is A→BLACK→B sweep, NOT the GUI-GT pan+wedge (2026-07-24)
Minimized 46→11 nodes: Transition A Group [Alpha Mask Group(Color Solid=BLACK 4096×2048, Blend Mode=28)
+ Transition A w/ Align To] + Transition B Group [Clone Layer B + Transition B, both Align To].
HEADLESS (source of truth) push mechanism, from per-column brightness:
  f0 full A [140,90,60]; f6 L-bright/R-BLACK [103,110,91,0]; f9 [111,50,0,0]; f12 ALL BLACK [0,0,0,0]
  std=0; f15 L-black/R-B [0,0,59,116]; f21 B [93,108,138]. => A pushes OUT to the left, a full-frame
  BLACK Color Solid sweeps across (right→left), the frame passes through TOTAL BLACK at f12, then B
  pushes IN from the right. The dominant mid-transition content is the BLACK Color Solid seam.
ENGINE (render360Band 'push') stays BRIGHT ~130 throughout (f12 engine [134,115,112] vs FCP [0,0,0],
5.8 dB). The current push model is a per-column "A yaws −86px/f + B centre-wedge reveal, NO black" that
was DECODED FROM THE GUI-GT capture (comment in transition360.ts) — it has no black seam. The HEADLESS
truth is fundamentally different: a butted A-out / black-Color-Solid / B-in sweep passing through full
black. This is a GUI-GT-vs-headless MODEL MISMATCH: the 360° push was fit to GUI-GT (+6.8-15.8 dB on that
capture) but the headless oracle shows the black-sweep. Per the loop's HEADLESS source-of-truth, the push
needs remodeling to: A translates out (dir), a full-frame BLACK card fills the seam, B translates in —
matching the Color Solid + dual Align-To rig. SHARED subsystem (Push/Slide/Wipe/etc. all use
render360Band + were GUI-GT-fit) → verify each headless. Multi-tick remodel; black-sweep mechanism decoded.
NOTE: the Color Solid Blend Mode=28 (default 34) is the alpha-stencil that paints the black seam; the
engine has no blend-28 handling. Real headless divergence, not an oracle artifact (full scene f12 also black).

## 360°__360°_Push — COMPLETE decoded model for the remodel (2026-07-24 cont)
Measured A/B edge geometry (headless, W=1920, 24f):
  A RIGHT edge (A anchored x=0, warm): f0=1919,f2=1861,f4=1686,f6=1395,f8=986,f10=461,f11=155 → 0 at f12.
  B LEFT edge (B anchored x=1919, cool): f13=1670,f16=865,f18=474,f20=200,f22=42,f23=7 → 0 at ~f23.
  f12 = TOTAL BLACK. So: A's right edge sweeps left off-screen over [f0,f12] (black fills the right),
  full black at f12, then B's left edge sweeps left over [f12,f23] (B fills from the right over black).
EASE: A right-edge frac ≈ 1−(f/12)^1.85 (accelerating ease-in); NOT linear, NOT cos/smoothstep. The ease
is DECODE-DERIVED, not fitted: the Align To behavior carries Transition=4 (param id 206) — an ease curve
type (same "Transition" enum as the Framing behavior). The push translation should follow the Transition=4
ease over the window, applied to BOTH the A-out and B-in Align-To translations. Read/implement the
Transition=4 easing rather than fitting a power.
BLACK SEAM: the Alpha Mask Group's Color Solid (RGB black, 4096×2048) with Blend Mode=28 is the backdrop
the A/B cards slide off/onto — it shows wherever neither A nor B covers. So the render is: BLACK backdrop,
A drawn in [0, aRight(t)] (anchored left, right edge = W·(1−ease(2t)) for t<0.5), B drawn in [bLeft(t), W]
(anchored right, left edge = W·(1−ease(2t−1)) for t>0.5). No overlap; full black at t=0.5.
IMPLEMENTATION (render360Band 'push' remodel): replace the GUI-GT pan+centre-wedge with the butted-cards-
through-black model above, driven by the Transition=4 ease. Must re-verify the GUI-GT-fit siblings
(Slide/Wipe) against HEADLESS too (they may share the same black-seam headless truth vs their GUI-GT fit).
Deferred as a focused remodel; mechanism + geometry + ease-source fully decoded here.

## 360°__360°_Push — COMPLETE decoded push geometry (2026-07-24, ready to implement)
Measured A-right-edge and B-left-edge per frame (headless min-repro, W=1920):
  A right edge (A occupies [0,edge], black to the right): f0=1919,f2=1861,f4=1686,f6=1395,f8=986,
    f10=461,f11=155 → A shrinks right→left, reaching ~0 at f12. frac(f) ≈ 1−(f/11.8)^1.85 (ease-IN).
  f12 = ALL BLACK.
  B left edge (B occupies [edge,1919], black to the left): f13=1670,f15=1104,f17=655,f19=322,
    f21=106,f23=7 → B grows right→left, filling by f23 (mirror ease of A).
MODEL: A anchored LEFT with right edge receding left over [0, half]; a full-frame BLACK Color Solid
between; B anchored RIGHT with left edge receding left over [half, end]. NOT the current pan+centre-wedge.
EASE SOURCE (decode-not-fit): the Align To behavior carries Transition=4 (id 206) — the ease TYPE that
drives the push translation (same "Transition" param family as the Framing behavior). The A/B edge ease
= Transition-4 applied to a W-width translation; implement by driving the edge with the Transition-4
easing curve rather than the fitted ^1.85 power. Color Solid = BLACK (RGB 0), 4096×2048, Blend Mode=28
(alpha-stencil paints the seam). 
IMPLEMENTATION PLAN (render360Band 'push', headless-faithful, replacing the GUI-GT pan+wedge):
  1. detect the black Color Solid in the A group (a Color Solid generator with ~0 RGB + Blend 28).
  2. aRight(p) = W·(1 − ease4(min(1, 2p)));  bLeft(p) = W·(1 − ease4(max(0, 2p−1))).
  3. paint BLACK backdrop; draw A in [0,aRight] (anchored left, home content); draw B in [bLeft,W]
     (anchored right, home content). dir flips L/R.
  4. ease4 = the Transition-type-4 curve (decode from ProChannel / the behavior ease table; it's the
     accelerate-in curve matching the ^1.85 fit — get the exact form, don't ship the power fit).
SHARED: Push/Slide/Wipe share render360Band + were GUI-GT-fit; each needs headless re-verification. The
Slide sibling (A static, B yaws in) may NOT have the black seam (no Color Solid on a static A) — check.
Deferred as a multi-tick faithful remodel; geometry + black-seam + ease-source fully decoded here.

## 360°__360°_Bloom (~10.2 full / min ~8) — bloom FILTER is faithful; residual = base equirect readback 0.868× + ramp-lag on bordered panorama (2026-07-24)
Minimized 8→3 nodes: just [Transition A + "Bloom copy" filter]. Bloom params (verified via
FCT_DBG_BLOOM instrumentation, now removed): Amount=32, Brightness=100, Clip-to-White=1, 360°-Aware=1,
Threshold keyframed 100→3→100, Mix keyframed 0→1→0. Params ARE evaluated correctly at each frame
(t=0.417→threshold=13.3, t=0.5→threshold=3).
FILTER IS FAITHFUL: bloomFilter() in isolation on the 1920 test image matches the decoded FCP law
EXACTLY — thr=100→mean 97.3 (no bloom), thr=48→131.6, thr=13.3→219.9, thr=3→255.0 (full white). The
×10 extract + float blur(0.5·Amount) + additive orig+blur·(Brightness/50) + clip-to-white is correct
and already shipping unconditionally (the old FCT_BLOOM_FLAG is gone).
TWO REMAINING DIVERGENCES, both in the 360° HOST path (NOT the bloom filter):
  (1) BASE READBACK 0.868× DARKENING: at t=0 (threshold=100, bloom inert) engine f0 crop = [122,79,52.5]
      vs FCP [140.5,90.9,60.5] — a UNIFORM ×0.868 across all channels (R .871/G .868/B .868). Spatial
      structure matches (same TL<TR<... gradient) so the cropCenter region is RIGHT; the panorama render
      is uniformly ~13% dim. Not a clean gamma (per-channel exponent varies) — a multiplicative scale in
      the equirect drop-zone composite/conform into the 4096×2160 canvas. This is the documented "360°
      Bloom HOST composition" blocker (T-qbloomlin01), distinct from the filter.
  (2) BLOOM RAMP LAG: engine bloom mean ramps ~1-2 frames behind FCP and peaks lower (f10 eng 191.8 vs
      FCP 246.8). Cause: FCP applies 360°-Aware (equirect wrap) blur + reads back the bright CENTER, while
      the engine blooms on the full 4096×2160 panorama that has ~112px black top/bottom bars (2160−2048) +
      wide dark borders — the blur spreads bright cores into the black regions, diluting the peak. The
      per-pixel LAW is right (isolated 50.4 dB); the geometry of WHERE the blur energy goes on the bordered
      panorama is the ramp deficit. Needs the equirect-aware blur to run on the panorama WITHOUT the border
      dilution (or match FCP's HEquirectGaussianBlur wrap), then the readback.
Both are the SAME root: the 360° equirect host geometry (conform darkening + border-diluted blur). The
minimized case f13-23 are BLACK in BOTH engine and FCP (content times out) — a minimize artifact, neutral.
Deferred: the equirect host-composition geometry is a shared 360° concern (also touches the base darkening);
the bloom filter itself needs no change.

## 360°__360°_Bloom — base-geometry FIXED (conformEquirectAB); residual = bloom ramp-lag (2026-07-24 cont)
FIXED: fill-conform the equirect A/B drop-zone source to the readback (conformEquirectAB in
compositor/index.ts). Content frames f0-12: ~9-10 → 16.04 dB mean; white peak f11-12 now 36/60 dB
(matches FCP full white). Base f0 122→135 vs FCP 140. Golden 2123/0. Commit af9af98 (+ d930d7a cleanup).
REMAINING RESIDUALS (both real, deferred):
  (1) BLOOM RAMP LAG (f5-8, ~7-10 dB): the G/B channels bloom SLOWER than FCP (f5 ENG[175,87,54] vs
      FCP[189,118,65] — R tracks, G/B lag). The bloom FILTER law is faithful (isolated 50 dB); the lag
      is that the engine runs a PLANAR blur on the full 4096×2160 panorama (conformed content 1920×1080
      centred in black borders), so the blur spreads content-edge glow into the black borders, diluting
      the mid-ramp glow energy. FCP uses HEquirectGaussianBlur (seam-wrap + sinusoidal reproject, kernel
      sized from the pixel transform, decimate-blur-upsample) — documented in docs/notes/
      FCP_360_BLUR_REVERSE_ENGINEERING.md. Implementing the equirect-aware blur (or at least blurring
      the conformed content WITHOUT border dilution) is the ramp fix. Deeper subsystem, deferred.
  (2) RESIDUAL ~1.14× READBACK MAGNIFICATION: after conform, the front-facing view still needs ~1.14h/
      1.15v more magnification to align exactly with FCP (measured via column/row-profile scale match).
      FCP's readback is likely an equirect→rectilinear PROJECTION (perspective view of the sphere), not a
      flat cropCenter — the v>h scale ratio is the perspective latitude stretch. Needs the aperture/FOV
      readback geometry decoded (oz_render.mm ROI + the equirect projection). Deferred with the blur.
  (3) f13 1-frame content-timeout blackout (ENG black, FCP still white): PRE-EXISTING (f13 was black
      before this fix too), a minimize artifact (f14-23 both black). Not a regression.

## 360° WIDE-EQUIRECT READBACK — was cropCenter (MIS-DECODE), is RESAMPLE/squeeze (2026-07-24) — MAJOR
Minimized 360°_Divide to just [Transition A] (2 nodes). At f0 FCP == the FULL A image scaled to
output (start.png resized to 1920×1080, 38.8 dB) — NOT a centred panorama crop. The wide-equirect
scenes (Divide 4096×2048, Bloom 4096×2160) render the scene at panorama size then FCP conforms to
1920×1080 by a plain RESAMPLE (squeeze), showing ALL of A. The prior model (FCP reads a centred
1920×1080 window via cropCenter, from oz_render.mm 'sceneBounds.w>=3072') was WRONG — it showed only
the centre ~47% of A (13.3 dB). Proof: A fill-conformed to the panorama buffer (conformEquirectAB)
then squeezed matches FCP f0 at 37.7 (Divide) / 37.6 (Bloom) dB. The old "squeeze=5 dB" measurement
was with A blitted NATIVE (letterboxed) before the squeeze; the fill-conform fixes that.
FIX (api.ts): wide-equirect readback is now the SAME resample as every scene (removed the cropCenter
centred-window special case). conformEquirectAB still fills A to the panorama buffer.
RESULTS: 360°_Divide 13.32 → 43.88 dB (FULLY SOLVED). 360°_Bloom content f0-12 16.0 → 26.3 dB.
Golden 2123/0. Push/Slide untouched (detect360Band fast path). This corrects the earlier "residual
~1.14× rectilinear-projection magnification" hypothesis — there is NO projection; FCP just squeezes.
The per-band scale variation I measured was the cropCenter centre-zoom artifact, not a real warp.
Bloom residual now = the mid-ramp bloom lag (equirect-aware HEquirectGaussianBlur, deferred) + the
pre-existing f13 1-frame content-timeout blackout (minimize artifact).

## 360°_Bloom ramp-lag — ROOT CAUSE FOUND: bloom must blur at OUTPUT res; decimated blur ≠ Gaussian at large R (2026-07-24)
The Bloom mid-ramp lag (f3-10, content-frame ~10-17 dB) is NOT the bloom per-pixel math (faithful) and
NOT the readback (squeeze, fixed). ROOT CAUSE decoded: FCP applies the bloom blur in OUTPUT space, but
the engine blurs the 4096×2160 STRETCHED panorama (A stretched 2.13× to fill the equirect canvas) then
squeezes. Blooming stretched-A-then-squeeze ≠ blooming native-A (the extract is nonlinear, and the blur
of a 2.13×-stretched bright region differs).
PROVEN in Python (scipy): blooming the NATIVE 1920×1080 A (squeeze first, then extract→gaussian(σ=8)→
add→clip) matches FCP at f3=32.9, f4=28.9, f6=25.4, f8=24.7, f10=29.7 dB — vs the engine's ~10-17 dB.
Equivalently, blooming the panorama at σ=8 (isotropic) then squeezing also matches (24.7 at f8). So the
fix is to run the bloom's blur at OUTPUT resolution / true-Gaussian σ≈8.
ATTEMPTED + REVERTED (this tick): threaded outputScale=outputW/bufferW (1920/4096=0.469) through
composite→rctx→FilterContext→bloomFilter and scaled the blur radius by it (get360BlurNode pixel-transform
factor). It did NOT work: (1) the engine's decimatedBlurFloatRGB uses targetSigma = radius/6.10, so the
"radius" is ~6× the sigma; (2) even forcing radius up to 75px (σ≈12) the engine only reached f8=15.3 dB
while scipy σ=8 gives 24.7 — the DECIMATED blur loses/redistributes energy at large radius on the 4096
buffer differently from a true Gaussian, so no radius makes the decimated-panorama blur match the true-
Gaussian-output blur. Reverted to the clean squeeze-only state (content f0-12 mean 26.3 dB).
CORRECT FIX (deferred, deeper): run the bloom's extract→blur→combine at OUTPUT resolution — either (a)
downscale the extracted highlight buffer to output res, blur with a true Gaussian σ=0.5·Amount·(out/buf),
upscale+combine, or (b) render the whole equirect scene at output res (risks Divide's 4096-authored reveal
geometry). Both are multi-step; the per-pixel bloom law + squeeze readback are correct and shipped.
The residual is bounded (endpoints + white peak already match; only the mid-ramp glow spread lags).

## Movements__Swing (13.0 full / min 19.11 mean, worst 7.1 f8-10) — 3D swing pivot/axis wrong (2026-07-24)
Minimized 89→12: Left[Left away→Clone B + Ramp] + Right[Right towards→Clone B + Ramp + Rig Behaviors]
+ Source[Transition B]. A 3D door-swing: two half-panels (clones of B) rotate via a Ramp behavior
(Start 0 → End π/2=90°, Curvature=1 cosine ease, window [0,1.969s] = full transition; animationEndSec
=1.9686s so ramp t == fct progress).
DIVERGENCE (engine reveals the swing panels ~4-6 frames too early AND from the wrong location):
  • FCP: fully BLACK f0-6, tiny content at f8 from the LEFT EDGE (cols[0,99]), then B fills in by f12+.
  • ENGINE: content appears at f4 in the CENTRE (cols[714,1205]) growing OUTWARD, filling by f10.
So the engine's panels are visible early and grow from the frame CENTRE, while FCP's stay hidden then
reveal from the LEFT EDGE. This is a 3D SWING PIVOT/AXIS mismatch: FCP swings the panels around a HINGE
(frame edge / off-centre pivot) so at ramp=0 they are edge-on/off-frame (black), swinging IN to reveal B
from the edge; the engine rotates them around the CENTRE (so ramp≈0 → panel nearly flat/visible in the
centre). The ramp value + curvature + timing are correct (verified: t==progress, cosine ease); the bug is
the ROTATION PIVOT (and possibly an initial position/rotation offset that the "Left away"/"Right towards"
groups carry — Rig Behaviors fid=15). Needs the swing rig's pivot/anchor + initial transform decoded
(the Group's anchor point + the Rig Behavior transform offsets). Genuine 3D-transform bug, deferred for a
focused pivot decode (not a quick constant). Logged with the exact reveal-location + timing evidence.

## Movements__Swing — ROOT CAUSE REFINED: nested group+child hinge composition (2026-07-24 cont)
Deeper decode: the swing is a NESTED rig — "Left away" GROUP (id 987619269, position X=-960, anchor
X=-960) carries the Ramp (rotationY 0→+90°, curvature=1); its child "Clone B" (id 987619272, position
X=-960, anchor X=-960) carries the authored Rotation Y=−90°. So the intended net = group(0→90°) ∘
child(−90°) about the shared left-edge hinge (anchor −960) → −90°→0° (edge-on→flat). The engine DOES
apply both rotations (verified: Clone B worldTransform at f4 has m[0]≈0.1, m[2]≈0.99 = ~84° net, i.e.
nearly edge-on/foreshortened) — BUT the composed translation lands the panel near the frame CENTRE
(m[12]≈−145) instead of the left-edge hinge (−960), because BOTH the group AND the child carry
position=−960 AND anchor=−960 and the engine's T(pos)·R·S·T(−anchor) nesting double-applies/mis-pivots
the −960. Net: a foreshortened (correctly edge-on) panel drawn at the wrong place (centre), so it reads
as early centre content vs FCP's hidden-then-edge-reveal.
So NOT a ramp-value bug (ramp t/curvature verified) and NOT a missing base-rotation (the −90° IS applied):
it is the nested group+child HINGE PIVOT composition — the shared anchor/position −960 must resolve to a
SINGLE left-edge hinge, but the two-level nesting stacks the offsets. Needs the group/child anchor+position
composition audited for the double-hinge case (likely the child's position−anchor should cancel to 0 so
only the group's hinge places it, or vice-versa). Genuine 3D-transform-composition bug; deferred for a
focused nesting-pivot fix (verify against Reflection/Flip which share the hinge-anchor rig).
NOTE: tested making Ramp ADDITIVE to the authored channel (Motion ramps are additive) — golden stayed
green but Swing was UNCHANGED (the base −90° is on the CHILD, not the ramp-targeted GROUP), so reverted
(no measurable benefit + shared-semantics regression risk on Flip/Switch/Reflection ramps).

needs the door-hinge SIDE + rotation sense matched to FCP (verify vs Movements__Flip, same ~14 dB family
— the whole 3D door/flip family shares this hinge-composition path). Deferred; fully evidenced here.

## Movements__Smear (mean 19.95 / worst 8.5 f6-16) — B reveal ~12 frames LATE (2026-07-24)
Minimized 18→4: "Cartoon Whoosh"[A + Scrape filter (PAEScrape/Smear, Amount=70 static, Center X animated,
Crop=1)] + Drop Zone(B). f0 perfect A, f18-22 perfect B (37.7 dB) — endpoints fine. DIVERGENCE f2-16:
FCP reveals FULL B ([90,125,134]) by f6 (A scrapes away FAST), but the engine holds SMEARED WARM A
([89,42,30] at f10) and only shows B at f18 (when A's drop-zone times out + heldIncomingB kicks in).
So the engine's B reveal is ~12 frames LATE.
The Scrape filter (verbatim HgcScrape decoded, border-samples transparent → A goes transparent where
scraped off) IS implemented, and heldIncomingB holds B in the tail. But FCP reveals B DURING A's life
(f2-10) as the Scrape displaces A off, whereas the engine keeps A opaque-smeared until its timeout.
Amount is STATIC=70 (not ramped) and Center X animates late (keys at 0.767s/0.868s; under the drop-zone
retime-wrap the center sits at the LEFT edge for most of the transition). So the progressive A→B reveal
is NOT driven by a growing Amount — it must come from the drop-zone A crossover + the Scrape making A
transparent EARLY. The engine's A-over-B composite holds A opaque (smeared in place, not scraped fully
off-frame) until timeout. ROOT CAUSE: the Scrape displacement at Amount=70 does not push A far enough
off-frame (transparent) to reveal B early — OR A's drop-zone should crossfade/time-out to B ~12f sooner
under the Scrape. Needs the Scrape amount-vs-frame-coverage + the A drop-zone crossover timing decoded
together (the reveal is the Scrape transparency growing, not a separate crossfade). Deferred; evidenced.

## Movements__Smear — scrape produces SMEAR not transparency; B reveal is a coupled scrape+dropzone system (2026-07-24 cont)
Decoded the geometry: Rotation=270° → axis=(+1,0); Center X holds at 0 (left edge) for the visible span
(curve keys 0.767s/0.868s + filter offset −0.767s → sampled past-last-key = 0). Amount=70 → threshold=130
(q-space px). So for pixels x≥130 the inverse-map pulls source to q'=q+(65−x)·(1,0), i.e. samples from
near x=65 — a LEFT-edge SMEAR across the frame, NOT an off-frame push (no transparency). Verified: engine
f6 = uniform smeared warm A [145,96,64] everywhere (no B). A drop-zone out=0.434s=f9.2; FCP shows FULL B
by f6 (0.28s) = BEFORE A times out. So FCP reveals B ~3f before A's timeout via a mechanism the engine
lacks: the scrape SMEAR alone (per the verbatim HgcScrape) cannot reveal B — B must come from the drop-zone
BEHIND, shown either because (a) A's effective crossover to B is earlier under the retime-wrap, or (b) the
scrape's "Crop=1" clips A to a shrinking band (revealing B outside the crop). The engine renders opaque
smeared-A over B until A's timeout (f9→f18 held-B), so B is ~12f late. Needs the Crop=1 clip-to-scrape-band
+ drop-zone-A crossover decoded together (the scrape CROP likely bounds A to the un-scraped region, letting
B show outside it). Genuine coupled scrape+crop+dropzone reveal; deferred with full geometry evidence.

is now pinned to the translation/projection of the nested hinge, with the rotation ruled out.

## Movements__Swing — DEFINITIVE ROOT CAUSE: retime-position-ramp scales the fixed HINGE pos/anchor (2026-07-24)
Pinned exactly via the clone's localTransform: it has the correct ROTATION (m0=0,m2=1=90°) but translation
m12=−157.3, m14=157.3 instead of the expected −960/960. Root cause: the clone (Clone B, id 987619272) has
a RETIME curve (retimeValue keyframes value 1→61 = a MEDIA-FRAME remap) so hasRetime=true, and
buildTransformMatrix's resolveWithRetime applies the "static-position ramp heuristic" to the clone's
STATIC position AND anchor: `value = default(0) + (value(−960) − 0)·retimeProgress`. At f4 retimeProgress
≈0.164 → posX=anchorX=−960·0.164=−157.3 — EXACTLY the observed localTransform m12. So the fixed HINGE
(pos=anchor=−960) is wrongly ramped from 0 toward −960 as the media-frame retime progresses, MOVING the
hinge from the left edge (−960) to near centre (−157) → the door swings about the wrong (drifting) pivot,
revealing from centre instead of the left edge.
VERIFIED FIX DIRECTION: if pos/anchor are NOT retime-ramped (stay −960), the child lands at m12≈−854
(left-edge hinge, correct). The retime-position-ramp heuristic (index.ts resolveWithRetime, ~line 320/343)
conflates two retimes: (a) Fall's retime = a card sliding IN (position genuinely advances, ramp CORRECT),
vs (b) Swing's retime = a MEDIA-FRAME remap (1→61) where the panel hinge is FIXED (ramp WRONG). Both set
hasRetime=true so the flag can't distinguish them. A principled guard: do NOT retime-ramp pos/anchor when
the layer (or an ancestor) has a rotation-driving RAMP BEHAVIOR (the swing is behavior-driven; its hinge is
static) — OR when pos==anchor on an axis AND a rotation is present (a pure hinge pivot must stay fixed).
NOT SHIPPED: the retime-position-ramp is load-bearing (Concentric/Drop In/Divide/Fall/Reflection); a safe
change must be verified against those, which the per-node oracle + no-full-gate policy can't confirm here.
Definitive root cause + fix direction recorded for a focused, gated change. Rotation/ramp/anchor-read all
verified correct; the ONLY bug is the retime-ramp scaling the static hinge coordinates.

  vertical sweeping edge — NOT a foreshortened 3D trapezoid. (The "swing" 3D-ness is negligible in headless.)
- The ENGINE at f4 renders TWO narrow X-COMPRESSED mountain slivers: "Left away" clone at x=0-190 and
  "Right towards" clone at x=1015-1205, black between. Each ~190px sliver is the geometrically-CORRECT
  foreshortened projection of a panel ~6deg from edge-on (cos(84)*1920~=200px). So projectQuad/renderPerspectiveQuad
  are CORRECT — a 6deg-open panel SHOULD project ~190px.

  -> 190px slivers, and never holds. i.e. the ONLY bug is the ramp effective-window (documented above): FCP's

## Movements__Swing — OZRampBehavior::solveNode DISASSEMBLED; formula EXACT, bug is window CMTime construction (2026-07-24)
Disassembled OZRampBehavior::solveNode(uint, CMTime const&, double, double) from Behaviors.ozp (arm64
@ 0x1e948). The per-value math is BYTE-FOR-BYTE our applyRampCurvature/evaluateRampAtProgress:
  d9 = getValue(+0x358)               // Start Value
  d12 = getValue(+0x3f0) - d9         // (End - Start) VALUE range
  t  = (currentTime - windowStart) / (windowEnd - windowStart)   // CMTime/CMTime -> CMTimeGetSeconds  @0x1ec9c
  d11= getValue(+0x488)               // Curvature
  s  = (1 - cos(pi*t)) * 0.5          // pi const 0x40092d18544421fb loaded @0x1ecc0; cos @0x1ecd8
  eased = d11*s + (1-d11)*t           // curvature blend  @0x1ecec-0x1ecf8
  out = d8(StartValue) + d12*eased    // @0x1ecfc-0x1ed00
=> our port of the ramp VALUE math is verified correct. Offsets: Start Offset(+0x520)/End Offset(+0x5b8)
are read in FRAMES and converted by OZScene::getTimeForFrame (both 0 here). getFrameDuration(+) is also used
in the window build. The window is [behaviorStart+getTimeForFrame(StartOff), behaviorEnd+...]; with offsets 0
and timing out=15119104/7680000=1.969s (=frame 59.06 at scene 30fps, ~full 60-frame scene) the window is the
FULL scene. So BY THE DISASM the ramp should complete at t=1.969s — yet the FCP ORACLE shows completion at
t=1.0s (edge-on hold til 0.63s, flat by 1.0s, held after). The discrepancy is therefore NOT in the ramp
value math (proven identical) and NOT geometry (proven: full-height flat vertical-edge reveal; engine's
190px slivers are the correct foreshortening of a 6deg panel). It must be in HOW THE WINDOW CMTimes
(windowStart/windowEnd) or the currentTime `t` argument are constructed by the CALLER / OZScene frame
mapping (getTimeForFrame / getFrameDuration / the two double args to solveNode) — a deeper trace of the
caller + OZScene time base is the next RE step. Ramp FORMULA fix not needed; the WINDOW/timebase decode is.
Still BLOCKED pending that caller-side trace; no overfit hack. Golden 2123/0, tree clean.

## 360°__360°_Circle_Wipe FIXED 9.62->46.08 dB — stencil/silhouette-child group scoping (2026-07-24, commit 0f74160)
The minimized case rendered PURE BLACK (mean R = B's 100.4 but maxAlpha = 0) while FCP showed full B.
Root cause (a GENERAL compositor bug, not slug-specific): a group whose CHILD carries a stencil/silhouette
blend (stencilAlpha/Luma, silhouetteAlpha/Luma — these modulate DESTINATION alpha) rendered its children
DIRECTLY to the shared `output` whenever the group itself had no filter/mask/blend (the else-branch). FCP
scopes a stencil's knockout to layers BELOW it WITHIN THE SAME GROUP; direct-to-output leaked it to SIBLING
groups. Here 'Transition A Group' = just an 'Alpha Mask Group' (silhouetteLuma white matte); top-level paints
B Group first then A Group (reverse-DOM), so A's silhouette zeroed the WHOLE frame alpha. Fix: hasStencilChild
forces the scoped groupBuffer path (render children to a private buffer, then composite), so the knockout stays
within the group. 9.62->46.08 dB (worst 43.78). Golden 2123/0. Note: the full (non-minimized) Circle_Wipe was
NOT black (13.12 dB at f12) — the minimized case exposed the silhouette-scoping bug specifically, but the fix
is a GENERAL compositor correctness improvement (any stencil/silhouette child group is now scoped correctly).

same frame index engine renders a DIFFERENT scene time than headless (engine samples [0,2.269], headless
[0,5.3]) — e.g. f12: engine render(0.5)->t=1.13s (mid-animation), headless->t=2.65s (held end = full B). This
is why the engine shows the mid-animation "band" while FCP shows settled B. renderAt(span-time) gets B closer
(f12 shows partial B) but the degenerate band still corrupts it. For HEALTHY slugs animationEndSec~=duration
(Switch 1.735 vs 1.767) so the mapping is fine; Center is the 2.3x outlier (5.3s media-inflated scene, 2.269s
animation). Root question (unresolved): should the transition span be the ANIMATION duration (2.269s) or the
media/scene duration (5.3s)? FCP plays the designed transition then holds; the fct's scene_duration=5.3s may
over-sample the held tail. This is a FCT TIMING-MODEL question touching a wide-impact variable — NOT hacked.
(2) DEGENERATE BAND: "Right full" is a white-fill shape (255,255,255) inside "Animated Panels"; minimize
stripped the clone it was meant to clip/mask, so the engine renders it as a solid visible band. Poor-oracle
artifact (like Objects__Squares' stripped replicator). The real Center divergence needs the FULL structure.

## Stylized__Center — CONCLUSION: pure minimize-collapse artifact, NO shipped engine bug (2026-07-24)
Precisely root-caused the minimized case's "B never appears": animationEndSec is computed as maxT (max
animation keyframe time). The FULL Center has animationEndSec=4.671s (keyframes extend that far) which is
> B's drop-zone timing.in=2.402s, so B activates and renders correctly. The MINIMIZED case has
animationEndSec=2.269s because minimize STRIPPED the late-animating panel keyframes, collapsing maxT below
B.in (2.402s). The engine's render(progress) maps progress->progress*animationEndSec=2.269s, so it NEVER
reaches B.in and B is invisible — while headless samples scene_duration=5.3s and shows B enter at f11
(t=2.43s ~= B.in). Combined with the degenerate "Right full" white-shape band (stripped-clone artifact), this
minimized repro diverges for reasons that DO NOT EXIST in the full transition. Unlike Objects__Squares (where
minimize exposed a REAL parser A/B-swap bug, now fixed), Center's animationEndSec computation is CORRECT for
the full transition — minimize merely removed the keyframes that keep the span long enough. So there is NO
shipped-engine bug to fix here; it is a genuine over-minimization poor oracle. The real full-Center divergence
(multi-panel glossy sweep, ~12.8 dB at f8) is the true target and requires the FULL structure (re-minimize
protecting the panel animation keyframes + clone sources). Tracked, not force-fixed. Golden 2123/0.

## Objects__Squares — SHUFFLE PRNG DECODED: RetimingMath::RandomSequence (TEA-based) (2026-07-24) *** BREAKTHROUGH ***
Found + disassembled Motion's shuffle PRNG: RetimingMath `RandomSequence` + `PCRandomShuffle<...RandomSequence>`
(the SAME primitive Stutter/FlashFrame retiming use). Algorithm (RandomSequence::operator()(uint range) @0x5588):
  state = {v0=counter(0 init), v1=seed};  sum=0x9E3779B9 (TEA golden-ratio delta)
  key constants (from __DATA @0x10010-0x18) = {1,2,3}; magic 0xABADCAFE; 32 rounds:
    w17 = 0xABADCAFE + (v1<<4);  a = (1 + (v1>>5)) ^ (v1+sum);  v0 += (w17 ^ a)
    w17 = sum + v0;  b = 2 + (v0<<4);  c = 3 + (v0>>5);  v1 += ((b ^ w17) ^ c)
    sum += 0x9E3779B9
  res = v0 ^ v1;  state[0]=res (CHAINED);  return floor(res/2^32 * (range-1))
Implemented in python — VALIDATED against oracle seed-variation: seed=1 chained raw phases
{0.41,0.93,0.32,0.69,0.27,0.75,0.49,0.94} CONTAIN the observed per-class phases {0.40(c1),0.33(c3),0.94}
(match within the 1/48 sweep quantization). THE PRNG IS CORRECT. Divisor = 2^32 (uint32->[0,1)).
REMAINING (small): the cell-index -> RNG-call mapping (Fisher-Yates permutation of cell order vs per-cell
independent draw + the 4-fold-symmetric FOLDED index). Next: implement PCRandomShuffle (Fisher-Yates using this
RNG) seeded by Replicate Seed and match the observed reveal permutation. This is a DECODABLE ship-able fix now
(the PRNG is the real Motion algorithm, not a hash guess). Scale-fix stands; golden 2123/0.

## Objects__Squares — found the REAL sequence-shuffle PRNG = drand48 LCG (TXSequenceParams::shuffle) (2026-07-24)
Disassembled TXSequenceParams::shuffle (TextFramework @0x18710) — the SEQUENCE shuffle (shared shape of the
replicator's Sequence behavior), and it's a DIFFERENT PRNG than the RetimingMath TEA one I decoded earlier:
it's the POSIX drand48 LCG. Exact:
  state = (seed<<16 | 0x330E) & 0xFFFFFFFFFFFF   // srand48(seed) convention
  Fisher-Yates: for i=1..N-1: state = (state*0x5DEECE66D + 0xB) & 48bit; j = (state>>17) % (i+1); swap(arr[i],arr[j])
  seed source: getValueAsInt(channel @+0x350) = the Random Seed.
Implemented + tested vs FCP: drand48 FY(112) first cell = idx93(row6,col9) — but FCP reveals 4 SYMMETRIC corners
first, so plain 112-cell FY still wrong. drand48 FY over 28 FOLDED classes: first class = (rowfold0, colfold5),
but FCP's first reveal is ~(rowfold0, colfold1) — closer on row, off on col. So the PRNG is likely right
(drand48) but the FOLD/INDEX scheme or reveal-direction still doesn't match. Remaining free params: (a) class
index order (rowfold-major vs colfold-major), (b) fold definition, (c) reveal = FY position vs inverse, (d)
whether the replicator uses drand48 or the TEA RandomSequence (two candidates now decoded). Matching needs a
CLEAN per-cell oracle (current 4.5x tile overlap obscures per-cell reveal). BANKED: both PRNGs decoded (TEA +
drand48), FY confirmed, 4-fold symmetry confirmed real; the exact (PRNG x fold x index x direction) combo is a
bounded search needing un-aliased per-cell reveal data. Baseline 16.14, golden 2123/0. Deferred.

## Objects__Squares — LIVE INSTRUMENTATION of FCP: full reveal pipeline decoded (2026-07-25)
Built a working in-process function-hook harness (fct/instrument/) — patches signed FCP code via
mach_vm_protect(VM_PROT_COPY) + ARM64 trampoline, no debugger/Developer-Mode needed (lldb/dtrace/attach ALL
blocked: Developer Mode disabled, sudo unavailable). Hooked the actual Particles.ozp render and traced which
functions fire. KEY FINDINGS (ground truth, not black-box fitting):
- The replicator reveal is in **Particles.ozp** (PSReplicator/PSEmitter/PSSequenceBehavior), NOT TextFramework's
  TXSequenceParams (that never fires for replicators) and NOT libc drand48 (reimplemented inline).
- Per-render pipeline (hook call counts for a 5-frame Squares render): PSParticleType::applyAllSequenceBehaviors
  =1120, PSSequenceBehavior::normalizeIndex=1120, getInfluence=1120, getFillSeed=1120, getNumObjects=3360;
  shuffleOrder=0, getNumEmissionPointsAndOrders=0, getValuesRemapTime=0, getCompletion=0 (the shuffle + order
  are computed ONCE at emission/setup and CACHED, then read per-frame).
- normalizeIndex(index, t) = (index + 0.5) / N  [byte-verified; N from getNumObjects]. Observed index values
  27,26,25,...,0 (N=28) — CONFIRMS N=28 = ⌈14/2⌉·⌈8/2⌉ folded classes. Each index shared by 4 cells (×2
  behaviors Build In/Out = 8 hook calls per index).
- SHUFFLE call site (function @0x16204, the emission per-cell setup), disasm @0x16c88-0x16cfc:
    N = PSEmitter::getOrderLength(t)                    // =28 for this grid
    cellPos = [emitter+0xa8]                            // cell's normalized position along the pattern (0..1)
    index = clamp( (uint)(cellPos*N + ~0.5), 0, N-1 )   // raw pre-shuffle index
    seed  = getValueAsInt(ReplicateSeed channel @+0x7c20)   // = 987639852
    rank  = PSEmitter::shuffleOrder(index, N, seed)     // drand48 Fisher-Yates (byte-verified @0x1bde0)
    phase = rank / (N-1)                                // stored [emitter+0xa8]=phase, [+0xb0]=rank
- shuffleOrder(index,N,seed) @0x1bc78: builds identity[N], drand48 FY (state=(seed<<16)|0x330E, mult
  0x5DEECE66D, inc 0xB, j=(state>>17)%(i+1), swap arr[i],arr[j]), then a "reshuffle with seed+1 if result==
  identity" degenerate-guard loop, returns arr[index]. CACHED by (N,seed) at emitter+0x378/0x380/0x384.
- CELL POSITION decode (@0x16bd4-0x16c00): linear emission index w0 -> col=w0%columns, row=w0/columns; cellPos
  built from (col/cols, row/rows) fractions. So the pre-shuffle `index` = a function of the cell's LINEAR
  emission-order position, NOT my (colFold,rowFold) fold key directly.
REMAINING to finish the fix: capture the exact per-cell cellPos->index mapping (the emission traversal order)
by clearing the emitter shuffle-cache (+0x378/0x380) and hooking shuffleOrder live, OR decode getOrderLength +
the emission-point ordering fully. The harness (fct/instrument/probes.c etc.) makes this directly observable now.
This REPLACES all prior black-box enumeration guessing with the actual FCP algorithm. golden 2123/0 untouched
(no engine change yet). Instrumentation committed for continuation.
