## Objects__Squares — reveal order = TEA-HASH shuffle (decoded from initPropertiesFromShape) (2026-07-25)
Disassembled PSEmitter::initPropertiesFromShape @0x16204 body (the per-cell emission that fills the reveal
order at particle+0xa8/+0xb0). The order fraction is computed by a **TEA (Tiny Encryption Algorithm) hash**,
NOT drand48 FY and NOT pure radial. Two TEA blocks @0x164f4 and @0x165b0 (one per axis / two-pass):
  delta = 0x9E3779B9 (TEA golden ratio); 32 rounds (w10 from -0x20 counting up to 0);
  keys k0,k1,k2 = *(0xe1000+0xc60), *(+0xc64), *(+0xc68);  state v0=w26, v1=w25/w28 (seeded 0x3712F987 region)
  per round: v0 += ((v1<<4)+k0) ^ (v1+sum) ^ ((v1>>5)+k1);  v1 += ((v0<<4)+k1') ^ (v0+sum) ^ ((v0>>5)+k2);
             sum += 0x9E3779B9
  hash = v1 ^ v0;  orderFrac = hash / 2^32  (divisor double 0x41EF_FFFFFFE00000 ~ 2^32)
  input v0/v1 seeded from per-cell vtable calls ([emitter+0x20], [emitter+0x258]) = cell index/coords + a
  per-cell counter (w26 accumulates).
Then genOrderLinear(orderFrac, reverseFlag, N, t, &outPos=+0xa8, &outIdx=+0xb0): reads a direction channel
(@0x56d8/0x57d8) and maps orderFrac -> outPos (with center-fold for mode 0: |1-frac| folded) and
outIdx = floor(outPos*N + 0.5 + eps). Then normalizeIndex((idx+0.5)/N) -> getInfluence reveal.
So: **each cell's reveal rank = TEA-hash(cellIndex, keys, seed) -> fraction -> fold/quantize to N shells.**
The earlier smooth-radial +0xa8 map was the genOrderLinear OUTPUT (folded), which LOOKS radial because the
center-fold + quantize collapses the hashed fractions into symmetric shells — but the ordering WITHIN is the
TEA hash (explains why onset didn't correlate with radial distance: corr 0.10).
REMAINING: extract the 3 TEA keys (0xe1c60 region; static read gave 0x4a7/0x80100000/0xd410 — likely needs
load-time reloc or correct section mapping) + the exact per-cell input (v0/v1 seeding from the vtable calls),
then reproduce TEA in TS. This is the REAL algorithm (TEA hash shuffle), decoded from the binary via
instrumentation-guided disasm. golden 2123/0.

## Objects__Squares — TEA keys are chained-fixup pointer slots -> need RUNTIME capture (2026-07-25)
The 3 "keys" at __DATA_CONST 0xe1c60 (ldr w8,[x8,#0xc60] etc) read statically as 0x4a7/0x80100000/0xd410... =
these are chained-fixup pointer slots (8-byte entries), so at RUNTIME they resolve to actual addresses; the
32-bit loads take the low bits. So the TEA "keys" are runtime-dependent values (possibly object/vtable
pointers used as key material, or I mis-mapped the adrp page). Static extraction insufficient — must capture at
runtime via the hook harness. NEXT (clear path): hook initPropertiesFromShape, and inside the hook read the 3
dwords at the RUNTIME resolved address (adrp base is knowable from the loaded Particles slide via dladdr on any
Particles symbol + 0xe1c60 vmaddr), plus capture the TEA input (the two vtable-call results seeding v0/v1) and
the output orderFrac (+0xa8 pre-genOrderLinear). With keys+input+output for a few cells, reproduce TEA exactly.
DEFINITIVE MECHANISM (decoded, durable): reveal order = TEA-hash(cellIndex; delta 0x9E3779B9; 32 rounds; 3
runtime keys; per-cell seeded state) -> orderFrac in [0,1) -> genOrderLinear(fold+quantize to N shells) ->
shell idx 0..N-1 -> normalizeIndex((idx+0.5)/N) -> getInfluence Build-In/Out ease. N=28 for 14x8. This is the

## Objects__Squares — SHUFFLE FULLY DECODED + VERIFIED across 4 seeds (2026-07-25) *** MILESTONE ***
Disassembled PSEmitter::shuffleOrder(index,N,seed) @0x1bc78 AND its call site @0x16c80. EXACT algorithm:
  shuffleOrder = drand48 Fisher-Yates, ASCENDING:
    X = ((seed & 0xFFFFFFFF) << 16) | 0x330E            # srand48
    for i in 1..N-1:  X = (X*0x5DEECE66D + 0xB) mod 2^48;  r=(X>>17)&0x7FFFFFFF;  j = r mod (i+1);  swap(p[i],p[j])
    return p[index]
  Call site: N = getOrderLength() = 28 (=ceil(14/2)*ceil(8/2) shells); seed = Replicate Seed channel (@0x7c20);
  index = genOrderLinear base-enumeration position; reveal-order = sort cells by perm[index].
VERIFICATION (controlled-input decode, gold standard): rendered the minimized case headless at 4 seeds
(111111111/222222222/333333333/987639852), measured per-cell reveal onset from FCP frames. Reveal forms 8
seed-invariant spatial BLOCKS (folded grid grouped cg=(cf+1)//2 in 0..3, rg=rf//2 in 0..1). Solved each block's
base index by 4-seed joint constraint => A(cg3,rg1... wait rep classes): baseidx { A:0,G:1,E:2,C:3,D:4,H:5,B:6,F:7 }.
The decoded FY(N=28,seed)[baseidx] reproduces ALL 4 seeds' block orders EXACTLY (8/8 each seed). This CONFIRMS
the shuffle is drand48-FY (not TEA — the TEA block at 0x164a8 is a DIFFERENT build-style branch not taken here).
Base-index spatial grid [rg][cg]: rg0=[7,6,4,3], rg1=[5,2,1,0] (center block=7=last, corner=0=first =>
outside-in-ish enumeration; exact genOrderLinear formula still to generalize for arbitrary grids).
NEXT (implementation): (1) generalize the base-enumeration (genOrderLinear for Build Style 2 / Origin 4 =
"grow from ...") to arbitrary Columns/Rows — either decode genOrderLinear's orderFraction math fully or capture
base order per grid; (2) implement drand48-FY(N=orderLength, seed=ReplicateSeed) in the engine's Sequence
Replicator reveal; (3) reveal each cell when globalProgress >= perm[baseIndex]/N (+ Build-In/Out ease from
getInfluence). All prior wired variants were REVERTED; engine still ships diagonal-sweep baseline (16.14 dB)
until base-enumeration generalized. golden 2123/0. seed variants: /tmp/sq_seed_*.motr; onsets /tmp/sq_onset_*.json.

## Objects__Squares — EXACT genOrderLinear-input TEA decoded (2026-07-25)
Fully decoded the TEA loop @0x165b0 (32 rounds, standard TEA/XTEA form). Register roles:
  v0 = w28 (init 0x3712F987), v1 = w26 (per-cell, from emitter.vtable[0x258]()), w0 = per-cell (from
  particle.vtable[0x20]()), sum = w12 (init 0x9E3779B9, += 0x9E3779B9/round), keys k0=w8=[0xe1c60],
  k1=w9=[0xe1c64], k2=w11=[0xe1c68] (runtime chained-fixup pointers).
  Round (all uint32):
    v1 += ((v0<<4) + w0) ^ (v0 + sum) ^ ((v0>>5) + k0)
    v0 += ((v1<<4) + w9?) ... symmetric: w13 = (v1<<4 + k1) ^ (v1 + sum) ^ ((v1>>5) + k2); v0 += w13
  After 32 rounds: hash = v0 ^ v1; orderFraction = hash / 2^32 (d1 = 0x41EFFFFFFFE00000 ≈ 2^32).
  Then genOrderLinear(orderFraction, ...) folds/quantizes -> base order index. (When Shuffle Order on, this
  base order is further permuted by drand48-FY(N=28,seed) — the DONE, 4-seed-verified layer.)
REMAINING to pin the GENERAL base closed form: capture the 3 runtime keys (k0,k1,k2 @0xe1c60/64/68) + the two
per-cell inputs (w0, v1) by hooking; replay this exact TEA; feed genOrderLinear. The TEA ALGORITHM is now fully
decoded — only the 3 runtime key VALUES + per-cell input accessors remain (both need a runtime hook that
currently perturbs the branch selection). Shipped drand48-FY fix (25.74 dB, 4-seed verified) holds and is the
correct shuffle mechanism; this TEA decode is the base-enumeration refinement for the residual. golden 2123/0.

## Objects__Squares — full pipeline traced via source instrumentation (2026-07-25, per vjeux)
Instrumented the ACTUAL functions (not guessing). Definitive findings:
- PSEmitter::calcOrderIndexFromOrderFraction (@0x15684, called from PSEmitter::initParticles @0x1474c): builds a
  std::__tree of all cells' orderFraction (dedup within 1e-7), then assigns each cell index = DENSE RANK of its
  orderFraction. Hooked it (following state-elem+0xe8 -> obj, orderFrac@+0xa8, index@+0xb0): captured exact
  orderFraction->index for all 112 cells. VERIFIED render particle+0xb0 == calcOrderIndex output 112/112.
- PSParticleType::applyAllSequenceBehaviors (@0x421e4) reads d8=[particle+0xb0] (the index) and passes it to
  PSSequenceBehavior::getValues -> (mode 4) getValuesRemapTime -> getCompletion -> normalizeIndex.
- normalizeIndex(index) = (index+0.5)/N (@0x59aac, N from vtable+0x2a0=getOrderLength). NO shuffle in it.
- getInfluence(index): monotonic threshold (high index reveals first at the frames it fired).
KEY CONTRADICTION (rigorously measured): the calcOrderIndex (radial dense-rank) does NOT match the real per-cell
screen onset (corr -0.05). index->mean-onset is SCRAMBLED (idx0->onset82, idx1->onset15, idx8->onset15,
idx9->onset154...). So a drand48-style SHUFFLE reorders the radial index into the reveal order, but it is baked
into a CACHED emission structure: getCompletion / getInfluence / shuffleOrder / genOrderLinear ALL show 0 render
calls (hooked) — FCP precomputes the reveal timing once at emission (before/around initParticles) and caches it;
the render just samples the cache. The shuffle (shuffleOrder @0x16cec in initPropertiesFromShape, drand48-FY,
seed=ReplicateSeed) is the reorder, applied at emission.
NET: cell -> orderFraction (radial, captured) -> dense-rank index (calcOrderIndex, captured, =render+0xb0) ->
[emission-cached drand48 shuffle] -> screen reveal order (measured from headless). The shuffle maps radial-index
-> reveal-rank; I have BOTH endpoints (radial index per cell AND real onset per cell) so the permutation is now
fully determined by DATA even though the in-emission shuffle call isn't render-hookable. NEXT: compose
cell->radial-index (calcOrderIndex formula) with the measured radial-index->reveal permutation, and check it
equals drand48-FY(N, seed) on the RADIAL INDEX (not on a block base) — that's the untested composition.
Shipped 25.74 block model retained + verified (golden 2123/0). All hooks in fct/instrument.

   (r1,c6) has b0=0 but onset 36, (r2,c2) has b0=27 but onset 78. So getInfluence(b0) monotonic does NOT gate
   the corner reveal — the corner reveals FIRST regardless of its (varied) b0.

   (factoryID 22) and an IMAGE MASK alongside the Sequence Replicator. The reveal = Sequence Replicator cell
   opacity (per-cell timing from b0) COMPOSITED WITH a spatial Gradient/Image-Mask wipe. The spatial mask
   explains the corner-first block structure; the sequence adds sub-timing.

   it's the shape-emission distance which is already non-trivial.

## Objects__Squares — TRUE RNG IS PCRandom::getRandTEAf (TEA), NOT drand48 (2026-07-25) *** MECHANISM CORRECTED ***
Hooked PCRandom::getRandTEAf (@0x15600, local symbol — hooked via shuffleOrder-relative base+0x15600). It fires
per-cell during emission (drand48 shuffleOrder fires 0×). Captured exact (w0,w1,w2)->value:
  getRandTEAf(cellIndex, subseed, ReplicateSeed) called TWICE per cell with subseeds 923990407 & 2206335123:
  teaf(0, 923990407, 987639852)=0.837318 ; teaf(0, 2206335123, 987639852)=0.229952 ; ... (all 112 cells x2 draws)
  w0 = cell index (0..111), w1 = a fixed sub-seed (2 of them, likely X/Y or pos/order channels), w2 = the
  Replicate Seed. This is THE seed-dependent per-cell RNG that produces the reveal scramble — explains why the
  seed sweep changed the reveal while drand48 shuffleOrder never runs.
getRandTEAf is a TEA hash (@0x15600, 32 rounds, delta 0x9E3779B9, keys k0/k1/k2 = [0xe1c60/64/68] = 1/2/3
confirmed at runtime): v0=w0, v1=w1, w2=per-round additive const in v0's update; returns (v0^v1)/4294967295.
  v0 += (w2 + (v1<<4)) ^ (sum + v1) ^ (k0 + (v1>>5))
  v1 += (sum + v0) ^ (k1 + (v0<<4)) ^ (k2 + (v0>>5)) ;  sum += delta   [sum init 0x9E3779B9]
My literal transcription does NOT yet reproduce the captured outputs (tried sum init 0/delta, incr pre/post, all
6 arg perms — none match teaf(0,923990407,987639852)=0.837318). So one detail is still off (round count off-by-
one? sum update position? a masking nuance?). The HOOK is the ground-truth oracle meanwhile.
NET CORRECTION: the Squares reveal RNG = getRandTEAf (TEA, seed-keyed), NOT drand48 Fisher-Yates. All prior
drand48-FY models (including shipped shuffledSequenceOrder) are the WRONG RNG family — they only coincidentally
correlate 0.76 via the spatial block structure. NEXT: (1) fix the TEA transcription to match the captured
oracle exactly (brute-force round-count/sum-schedule against the 112×2 captured values); (2) decode how the 2
per-cell TEA draws build the orderFraction/reveal order; (3) implement getRandTEAf in the engine + wire the
reveal. Shipped 25.74 retained + verified (golden 2123/0). Captured data: /tmp/teaf_trace.txt (112×2 exact
TEA input->output pairs) — a complete oracle for validating the reimplementation.

## Objects__Squares — getRandTEAf TEA transcription VERIFIED byte-exact + hook adrp-bug found (2026-07-25) ***
Called getRandTEAf DIRECTLY (no hook) via ctypes at its real address: getRandTEAf(0,0,0)=0.76410444,
(1,0,0)=0.71445301, (0,1,0)=0.08953783, (0,0,1)=0.02359201, (1,1,1)=0.25827765, (0,923990407,987639852)=
0.18888908. My TEA reimplementation (keys k0/k1/k2=1/2/3, 32 rounds, delta 0x9E3779B9, sum init=delta,
sum+=delta at END; v0 += (w2+(v1<<4))^(sum+v1)^(k0+(v1>>5)); v1 += (sum+v0)^(k1+(v0<<4))^(k2+(v0>>5)); return
(v0^v1)/4294967295) reproduces ALL 6 anchors EXACTLY. => getRandTEAf is DECODED byte-exact.
INSTRUMENTATION BUG FOUND: hooking getRandTEAf gave WRONG values (0.837/0.277 for the same input) because its
prologue is PC-relative adrp (loads keys from 0xe1000 page); my trampoline COPIES those first 4 instrs to a
different page, breaking the adrp target -> corrupted key reads. The DIRECT call (intact adrp at real address) is
the truth. LESSON: never copy a PC-relative (adrp/adr/ldr-literal) prologue into a trampoline — hook after the
PC-relative block or relocate the adrp. (Applies to all fct/instrument hooks on functions starting with adrp.)
DECODED MECHANISM (corrected + verified): per-cell orderFraction is TEA-random via getRandTEAf(cellIdx, subseed,
ReplicateSeed) — seed-keyed, NOT geometric (orderFrac corr with radial = only 0.16). calcOrderIndexFromOrderFraction
dense-ranks orderFraction -> reveal index -> normalizeIndex((i+0.5)/N) -> getInfluence timing. The reveal is a
SEED-KEYED TEA-RANDOM order (drand48-FY fires 0×; all prior drand48 models WRONG). Shipped block model's 0.76
corr is just residual spatial clustering.
REMAINING (well-defined): capture the exact per-cell TEA inputs (subseeds + how the 2 draws build orderFraction)
via a NON-corrupting hook (hook the CALLER of getRandTEAf, or patch after its adrp prologue). Then: orderFraction
= f(getRandTEAf draws), dense-rank, reveal. TEA is byte-exact and ready to port to the engine. Shipped 25.74
retained + verified (golden 2123/0). Oracle: direct-call anchors + calcord2 orderFractions.

## Objects__Squares — orderFraction = center-distance of TEA-JITTERED position (2026-07-25) ***FULL MODEL***

differ enough). Shipped block model's reveal order corr with FCP true = only 0.10 -> real headroom.

draws map to the (x,y) position offset). Then: engine computes jittered pos -> center-dist orderFraction ->

## Objects__Squares — REVEAL PATH FULLY TRACED: getValuesWithInfluence + getInfluence(calcIndex) (2026-07-26) ***
Resolved getValues' dispatch (runtime vtable hook): vtable@0x128 = OZBehavior::isActiveAtTime (activity check,
returns bool). getValues (0x58e20): if active, reads channel@0x1118; ==4 → getValuesRemapTime, ELSE →
getValuesWithInfluence (0x57ec8). Since getValuesRemapTime fires 0×, channel@0x1118 ≠ 4, so the reveal path is
**getValuesWithInfluence**.
getValuesWithInfluence: calls getInfluence(CMTime, idx) → influence ∈ [0,1], then samples the behavior's target
Color (channel@0x4b0, ≈black RGB 0.0078) and does **PCColor::mix(targetColor, influence)** on the tile's mask
color. influence=0 → tile mask unchanged (SHOWN → revealed); influence=1 → mask→black (HIDDEN → not revealed).
getInfluence (0x57a28) HOOKED at frac 0.3: receives INTEGER calcIndex 0..27 (the dense-rank, NOT [0,1]), and is
MONOTONIC: idx≤7 → influence 0.0 (revealed), idx8 → 0.488, idx≥9 → influence 1.0 (hidden). So at frac 0.3, the
LOW-calcIndex cells (idx≤7) are revealed, high-calcIndex hidden. calcIndex = ascending dense-rank of
orderFraction (center-dist): idx0=center, idx27=corner. => reveal is CENTER-FIRST (center-out).
THE PERSISTENT CONTRADICTION: every function in the path is monotonic in calcIndex=center-dist-rank, so reveal
should be pure center-out. But the SCREEN (headless==ozengine) shows outer-cols+center first, middle-cols(3,4,9,
10) last — NOT center-out. corr(center-dist-rank, onset) = −0.16. The ONLY unproven link left: the calcIndex
assigned to each SCREEN cell ≠ dense-rank of that cell's center-distance. i.e. the emission→screen-position map
scrambles which cell gets which calcIndex. (calcpos: emission id0 has orderFraction 0.869 = cd(1,0), NOT the
corner cd(0,0)=1.0 → emission id ≠ screen row-major, despite genPos/genOrder being row-major in lockstep at
EMISSION. The particle STORED at emission-slot-k is DRAWN at a different screen cell.)
NEXT: hook applyAllSequenceBehaviors (PSParticleType 0x421e4 — the 0x7a720 PSPaintPType variant tail-calls & my
trampoline missed it; use 25-char symbol _ZN14PSParticleType25applyAllSequenceBehaviors...) to read particle
POSITION + calcIndex together = the definitive screen→calcIndex map. That map IS the "scramble" and the last
piece. Shipped block model 25.74 retained (RULE 2: no fitted guess shipped). golden 2123/0.

LAST PIECE: the emission-slot→screen-cell permutation. NEXT: hook calcOrderIndexFromOrderFraction (0x15684) —
it iterates the sim array (elem+0xe8→obj), reads obj+0xa8 (orderFraction); the obj HAS position. Read obj

CONTRADICTION (fully characterized, still open): the per-screen-cell calcIdx I read from the binary (calcidx3,
via calcOrderIndex sim-array hook) gives corr -0.09 with headless onset — even though reveal=ascending calcIdx.
So calcidx3's screen->calcIdx map is WRONG: its position field (elem+0x0, range +-1899 vs screen +-960) is in a
scrambled/2x coordinate space; orderFraction != center-dist of that position (err 0.24). The first-revealing
cells are (0,1),(0,2),(1,1),(1,2)+mirrors (top/bottom-edge, inset 1 col) — NOT center, NOT corner. Reveal is a
deterministic 8-block scramble, verified by BOTH pixels and the getInfluence oracle:
  BLOCK ORDER (cg,rg): [(1,0),(0,0),(0,1),(3,1),(1,1),(2,1),(3,0),(2,0)]  (cg={0,13}/{1,2}/{3,4}/{5-8}, rg=outer/inner)
NOT drand48-FY(8) (tested all seeds/shifts/bases/maps), NOT center-dist, NOT |of-0.5| (corr -0.54 close but not exact).
ROOT BLOCKER: I cannot get a correct screen-cell -> calcIdx map from the binary — every position field read is
in a wrong space (emitter/world 2x, not final screen). The order-grid (where genOrderFilledRect assigns center-
dist) maps to screen via genPos in a way I can't read correctly. Hardcoding the 14x8 block order would be
overfit (RULE 2). 
STATE: mechanism fully decoded EXCEPT the order-grid->screen coordinate map. Shipped block model (0.79 corr,

correct screen-position field for each particle, or the genPos index->screen formula for Build Style=2/Arrangement=1).

## Objects__Squares — getInfluence ORACLE proves reveal = ascending calcIdx (monotonic); block order verified (2026-07-26) ***
Fixed the multi-frame getInfluence capture (influence goes 1→0 as reveal front passes; 8 cells share each calcIdx;
onset = frame influence drops <0.5). RESULT: reveal onset is EXACTLY monotonic in calcIdx — idx0→f1, idx1→f2,
... idx27→f23 (clean linear). So reveal = ascending calcIdx, and calcIdx = dense-rank of orderFraction. influence
1→0: influence=1 tile black/hidden, influence=0 tile shown/REVEALED. Low calcIdx reveals first.
Composing the getInfluence oracle (calcIdx→onset) with headless pixels (screen→onset) gives the TRUE per-screen-
cell calcIdx = the reveal-tier grid. Expanded to the 8-block table (cg=col-group {0}/{1,2}/{3,4}/{5-8},
rg=row-group outer/inner):
  reveal order = [(1,0),(0,0),(0,1),(3,1),(1,1),(2,1),(3,0),(2,0)]  (VERIFIED 2 ways: pixels + oracle).
This block order matches the true reveal at Spearman 0.98 (vs current TS radial+drand48 model 0.75).
BUT: calcidx3 (binary calcOrderIndex hook) per-screen-cell calcIdx gives corr −0.09 with reveal — because its
elem-position read (±1899, 2× screen scale, orderFraction≠center-dist of it) is a WRONG/scrambled coordinate
space. So the binary's per-cell calcIdx (as I read it) ≠ the reveal order; the authoritative source is the
oracle+pixel block table.
GENERAL RULE SEARCH (RULE 2 — no overfit): the block order is close to "descending |meanOF−0.5|" (blocks whose
orderFraction is farthest from 0.5 reveal first — a both-ends-inward sweep) — corr 0.65, but NOT exact. Cell-level
|of−0.5| = 0.54. Not a clean closed form yet. NOT drand48 (shuffleOrder fires 0×, FY8/FY28 don't match).
DID NOT hardcode the 8-block table (would overfit 14×8). The exact block-order generating formula remains the
open piece; it's deterministic, ~both-ends-inward, but the precise within-half order isn't decoded.
Shipped block model (radial+drand48, 0.75 cell-corr, 25.74 dB) RETAINED. golden 2123/0.
NEXT: decode the exact both-ends-inward block-order formula (why cg2 reveals last, exact within-half order), OR
find the binary function that emits this order (the reveal index the render truly uses).

## Objects__Squares — calcOrderIndex output ≠ render reveal order, even in-engine (2026-07-26) ***definitive wall***
Captured calcOrderIndexFromOrderFraction sim-array with REAL local positions (elem+0x0/+0x8): local grid 14×8,

onset) = −0.07. So even in the very engine that computed calcOrderIndex, the per-screen-cell calcIdx does NOT
predict the reveal. This rules out headless-vs-ozengine and grid-alignment as causes.

orderFraction) that is NOT the reveal order used at render. getInfluence IS monotonic in the index the render
applier (0x42214 `ldr d8,[particle+0xb0]`) passes it — so that +0xb0 at RENDER differs from the +0xb0
calcOrderIndex wrote (captured by calcidx3). There is a REASSIGNMENT of particle+0xb0 between calcOrderIndex and
the draw, at a stage not yet hooked (likely the sim/draw pipeline reorders particles or recomputes the index,
possibly tied to Depth Ordered=1 / the OZSimStateArray→draw copy).

calcOrderIndex dense-rank, getValues→getValuesWithInfluence→getInfluence(+0xb0, monotonic)→PCColor::mix(black).
The ONE gap: the +0xb0 reassignment between emission-calcOrderIndex and render. Authoritative reveal order (8-block
table, oracle+pixels, Spearman 0.98 vs true) is banked.
Shipped block model (0.75 cell-corr, 25.74 dB) RETAINED. golden 2123/0. NEXT: hook the sim→draw pipeline to
capture the render-time +0xb0 per screen cell (find where +0xb0 is reassigned after calcOrderIndex).

Built ginfenc hook: override getInfluence to return idx/27, so each tile's mask-darkening encodes its calcIdx in

## Objects__Squares — CEILING TEST: verified block order REGRESSES to 14.6 dB (2026-07-26) ***critical negative result***
Wired the "verified" 8-block reveal order [(1,0),(0,0),(0,1),(3,1),(1,1),(2,1),(3,0),(2,0)] (0.98 rank-corr with
my measured onset) into shuffledSequenceOrder as a ceiling test → min-gen = 14.6 dB, WORSE than shipped 25.74.
Reverted. This proves my "verified order" block→SCREEN-cell mapping (cg=(cf+1)>>1, rg=rf>>1 in SCREEN space) is
WRONG: the 0.98 corr was self-consistent on a mis-aligned grid, but assigning that order to screen cells via
screen-space fold-blocks does not match actual pixels. The shipped radial+drand48 model (0.75 rank-corr) produces
BETTER pixels — because either its reveal TIMING is closer, or its block→screen assignment happens to align
better.
This confirms the earlier finding that LOCAL grid ≠ SCREEN grid: I cannot map the reveal order to screen cells
correctly without decoding the local→screen (replicator arrangement / 3D projection) transform. Every screen-
space block assignment I try is mis-aligned.
CONCLUSION: the shipped block model (25.74 dB) is the best VERIFIED-against-pixels model. Improving it requires
the local→screen coordinate map, which needs the 3D-projection/genPos decode (deep, deferred). All order-analysis
done in screen-space fold-blocks is unreliable due to this mis-alignment. No change shipped. golden 2123/0.
KEY LESSON: rank-correlation of a reveal ORDER against measured onset is NOT sufficient — the block→screen-cell
BINDING must be pixel-verified (via min-gen), because a self-consistent order on a mis-aligned grid scores worse.

## Objects__Squares — DECISION: bank deep investigation, mechanism decoded but blocked on 3D-projection map (2026-07-26)
After exhaustive RE this session, the Squares reveal is decoded to the mechanism level:

 - calcIdx = dense-rank of orderFraction (local replicator space)
 - getInfluence → PCColor::mix(black, influence); influence 1→0, 0=revealed

## 2026-07-26 — Replicator single-axis centering FIX + Squares 1×1 is a POOR ORACLE
SHIPPED FIX (committed d2a1fe5, pushed): generateInstances grid path — a single-row/column grid
(cols==1 or rows==1) now centers its lone cell on the ORIGIN. Was `startX=-sizeWidth/2` (top-left
corner); Motion centers the grid, and with only one cell on an axis there is no span so it belongs
at 0. Fix: `startX = cols>1 ? -sizeWidth/2 : 0; startY = rowCount>1 ? sizeHeight/2 : 0`. Only affects
1×N/N×1/1×1 grids; multi-cell grids byte-unchanged. Verified vs headless FCP: engine tile now renders
at frame centre (was left-edge). golden 2123/0, tsc clean, Objects__Squares 14×8 unchanged (25.74 dB).

POOR-ORACLE FINDING (do NOT re-chase): the aggressive minimizer reduced Objects__Squares to a 1×1
grid. After the centering fix the 1×1 tile is centered but ~2.17× too SMALL (engine 136px vs FCP
295px final). ROOT CAUSE of the residual is that hand-editing Columns/Rows on the 14×8 template is a
POOR ORACLE — FCP special-cases degenerate grids:
  - 1×1 → 1 tile at fixed 295px (final-frame)
  - 2×1 → renders BLACK (nothing)
  - 4×1 → only 2 of 4 tiles render, each 295px
FCP's tile is ALWAYS a fixed 295px regardless of grid; the real 14×8 tiles TESSELLATE correctly
(engine & FCP both mean~121 at t=1, 35.8 dB fully-revealed). So the 1×1 size mismatch is an
over-minimization artifact, NOT a shipped-path bug. The faithful repro is the real 14×8.

PER-TILE REVEAL IS BINARY (re-confirmed by fine-t sweep): a center tile SNAPS 0→98.8 between t=0.175
and t=0.180 (single frame, NO fade). The shipped block model's `front >= v/n ? 1 : 0` binary step is
CORRECT per-tile behavior. The residual 14×8 divergence (16.8 dB at t=0.2-0.6) is purely the reveal
ORDER/permutation (which tile snaps at which t) — the Ozone-core-draw-loop reveal index that is
decoupled from the Particles calcIdx (Spearman −0.07, characterized 2026-07-26, DEAD END without GPU
draw-loop decode). Wavefront col-coverage at t=0.3: FCP has partial cols 4/9 (80/89px) the engine
misses AND engine over-reveals col 2 (503 vs 380) — an order-permutation difference, not softness.

## 2026-07-26 (cont) — Squares tile SIZE decoded (295px) but COUPLED to reveal (can't ship alone)
DECODE (clean, unconfounded — varied cell node Scale id=105 in FCP): FCP tileW_final = vertRange ×
cellScale × nodeScale EXACTLY: ns 0.5→73px, 1.0→142px, 2.11→295px (predicted 69/138/292). tileW is
INDEPENDENT of grid cols/rows/sizeWidth. The engine renders tiles at HALF (137px) because the scene
rasterizes at native canvas W=4096 and api.ts RESAMPLES ÷(4096/1920)=÷2.13 to output, cancelling one
nodeScale=rsx factor. FCP's 295px tiles OVERLAP the ~112px grid spacing 2.6×; the engine's 137px tiles
merely ABUT — so each engine tile covers ¼ the area FCP shows mid-reveal (confirmed: engine "corner
tiles" measured 275px were actually 2 merged 137px tiles).
TRIED THE FIX (multiply tile basis by W/outputRefWidth to survive resample → tile_final =
verts×cellScale×nodeScale). tsc clean, but it REGRESSED Squares 16.75→11.51 dB: with correct 295px
tiles the engine OVER-reveals (t=0.2 mean 45→78 vs FCP 45). REVERTED. Root reason: the shipped block
model (137px tiles + block reveal, 25.74 dB) matches FCP's COVERAGE FRACTION by revealing MORE small
tiles; correct 295px tiles need the correct (FEWER-tile) Ozone reveal SIMULTANEOUSLY — you cannot fix
tile size without the matching reveal order/count. FCP coverage steps in plateaus: 0→14→36→36→52→52
→52→82→82→86→100% (quantized to keyframe times). FCP reveal PATTERN (grid-sampled, overlap-corrupted):
4 corner 2×2 blocks first (t=0.1), then a center 6×4 block (t=0.2), growing symmetrically, gaps last.
This is NOT center-distance (farthest-first gives corners but no simultaneous center) and NOT a diagonal.
The grid-sample is corrupted by 295px tile overlap so the "cells" aren't distinct tile reveals — this is
the SAME Ozone-core-draw-loop reveal-index dead-end (Spearman −0.07 vs Particles calcIdx). CONCLUSION:
tile-size decode is CORRECT and banked; shipping it requires the coupled reveal decode (GPU draw loop),
still a dead-end. Squares stays at 25.74 dB (block model). Do NOT ship tile-size alone.

## 2026-07-26 (cont) — Slide_In gradient: Y-DOWN direction DECODED; brightness needs colourspace decode
DECODED (verified): Motion's Gradient generator (pluginUUID 40091D89) authors Start/End endpoints in
TEXTURE space with Y +DOWN (top-centre origin), NOT the canvas +up convention renderLinearGradient
uses. Flipping to `cy=(y+0.5)-hh` makes BOTH the minimized and FULL Slide_In render light@top→dark@
bottom, matching FCP's trend (was inverted). The generator composites blend=normal, opacity=1, clipped
by ONE rig-selected mask ("...down" variant, Direction=1) over Transition B — so at col960 the visible
colour IS the pure gradient (no B blend), making it a clean colour oracle.
BLOCKER (do NOT ship the Y-flip alone): (a) it REGRESSES the minimized min-PSNR gate (19.2→13.5) — the
PRE-flip inverted gradient accidentally CROSSED FCP's curve, giving lower MSE; the flip is directionally
correct but exposes a brightness gap. (b) Engine gradient is ~80 too BRIGHT. Best model so far
(Y-down + interpolate RAW sRGB stops + srgbToLinear the OUTPUT) matches G/B channels within ~15 but RED
is consistently too HIGH: full col960 row540 FCP=[4,105,132] vs model[60,118,121]; row830 FCP=[0,90,119]
vs [33,89,92]. FCP's RED collapses toward 0 at the dark end faster than any lerp+linearize model — points
to a colourspace/gamma factor (stops carry Gamma 2.2 + Color Space id=11; generator HDR White Level 0.95)
not yet decoded. NEXT: decode whether stops interpolate in linear (loc0l/loc1l) vs sRGB, and how the
per-stop Gamma 2.2 + HDR White Level 0.95 factor in — ship Y-down + the colour decode TOGETHER as one
complete fix (decode-don't-fit; do not ship the partial flip).

## 2026-07-26 (cont) — Slide_In midpoint curve DECODED: f' ≈ t^2 (Interpolation param, not Middle)
Decoded the exact gradient interpolation curve by reversing FCP's implied blend fraction f' (sRGB space)
from the isolated gradient col960 (loc0=[0,158,181], loc1=[255,255,255] linked):
  row0 t=0.806 f'=0.774; row160 t=0.687 f'=0.508; row320 t=0.567 f'=0.308; row640 t=0.328 f'=0.083.
The curve is f' ≈ t^EXPONENT with EXPONENT ≈ 2.0 (t^2 fits err=179; t^1.8 err=182; Middle-derived
t^2.348 err=298; pure linear err=498). So the shipped per-stop MIDDLE power (ln0.5/lnM = 2.348 for
M=0.744) is CLOSE but the true exponent is ~2.0 — which matches the gradient's "Interpolation" param
(id=100, value=2). HYPOTHESIS: Interpolation=2 sets a gamma/power-2 fraction curve, and the Middle
either doesn't apply or applies differently than the standard f^(ln0.5/lnM). NOT yet shipped: need a
SECOND gradient with different Middle+Interpolation to disambiguate (is exponent == Interpolation value?
does Middle still matter?). Interpolation in sRGB space then linearize-output is CONFIRMED (linear-space
interp gives negative f' below mid-frame — impossible). Residual undershoot at the very top (t=0.806:
t^2=0.65 vs FCP 0.774) may be a panel-edge/second-element effect. Current shipped Middle-power is a
reasonable approximation (Slide_In already 8.34→21.16 dB); refining to Interpolation-power-2 is a small
gain (err 298→179) but needs the enum semantics decoded first (decode-don't-fit). DEFER pending a 2nd
gradient sample to confirm Interpolation→exponent mapping.

## 2026-07-26 (cont) — Light Sweep TIMELINE-COMPRESSION fixed (animationEndSec 1.301→1.433)
ROOT CAUSE of the f12 "over-bright" divergence was a TIMELINE MISALIGNMENT, not a rendering bug: the
engine's animationEndSec was 1.301s (last spatial keyframe) but the authored scene span is 1.433s
(<duration>43</duration>/30fps). The s24/min-score harness feeds NORMALIZED t and each engine multiplies
by its own end — FCP-headless by the span (1.433), the TS engine by animationEndSec (1.301) — so at the
same normalized t the two rendered DIFFERENT scene times (~0.07s apart), mis-aligning every frame.
The B-hold fix (731f517) should have caught it but required B.out<=span; Light Sweep's B.out=1.47 >
span 1.433 (padded editor timeline) so it was excluded. FIX (661981d): gate the B-hold on its START
within span (inS<span) and CLAMP out to span (held=min(outS,span)). animationEndSec 1.301→1.433 → tail
now settles on B matching FCP (f23 center [204,196,190] == FCP [203,196,189], 23.7 dB). Center unchanged.
RESIDUAL (now correctly aligned): mid-transition (f10-f18, t≈0.6-1.07) the engine is TOO BRIGHT (f18
en 203 vs FCP 146) — the lens-flare .mov screen-overlay and/or softLight gradient generators over-
brighten. Worst ~9.8 dB. Separate follow-up (needs the flare fade / screen-blend / softLight-gradient
decode). NOTE the minimized Stylized__Light_Sweep case (struct 160→2, 6.92 dB) is likely OVER-reduced

     FCP's getScaleForImage≠1 (the "A" plate is a conformed drop-zone), the fold could push content
     OFF-FRAME → transparent border → B shows through with the gradient. This would make A's smear
     region transparent (not opaque col-65), letting the existing order (A on top) reveal B correctly.
DISAMBIGUATION NEEDED: check FCP's smeared-A ALONE (disable B) at f10 — if it's TRANSPARENT/gradient in
x≥130 → hypothesis (b) (scale); if it's opaque col-65 like the engine → hypothesis (a) (order). That
single FCP render (B disabled) decides the fix. Then ship the principled version. The Scrape math + f0
match are correct; this is the last A→B handoff piece. Near-shippable, one decode step remaining.

Rendered FCP's smeared-A ALONE (Drop Zone B disabled) at f10: rgb mean=0.0, ALPHA mean=0.0 EVERYWHERE

## 2026-07-26 (cont) — Smear MECHANISM pinned: A-plate retimeValue extrapolation=1 (engine holds, FCP drops)
FINAL Smear mechanism: the "A" plate (transitionA) carries a retimeValue {type1, keyframes t=0→frame1,
t=1.234s→frame38, retimingExtrapolation=1, offset=-0.767}. So A is a RETIMED clip playing media frames
1→38 across the transition, with WRAP/loop extrapolation. At f10 FCP renders A fully TRANSPARENT (alpha
0) — its retimed frame has advanced past the A content and FCP drops it — while the ENGINE holds A opaque
(the wrapToA/persistent-A path keeps it visible). The Drop-Zone B has the SAME structure (retimeValue
frames 1→15, extrapolation=1, in=0.033). So the A→B handoff is driven by the RETIME VALUE curves: FCP
advances each plate's clip-frame and drops A when its retimed frame exhausts, revealing B. The engine's
retime-extrapolation=1 handling (ectx.wrapToA in evaluator/index.ts) keeps A visible/persistent instead
of dropping it. FIX LOCUS: the transitionA retime-extrapolation visibility — when a retimingExtrapolation
=1 transitionA plate's retimed frame passes its content end, it should go TRANSPARENT (not persist) so
the incoming B shows. NEXT: decode the exact frame→visibility rule (frame 38 at t=1.234s; at f10 t=0.167
the retimed frame is ~1+(0.167+0.767)/1.234*37≈29 — check if FCP drops A when frame exceeds a threshold).
Bounded retime-subsystem fix, mechanism fully pinned. golden 2123/0, tsc clean, working tree clean.

## 2026-07-26 (SYSTEMIC) — Drop-zone A→B handoff is a SHARED subsystem (Center/Light_Sweep/Smear)
Smear's wrapToA=false (no filled-shape overlay), holdIncomingB/filterRevealSettleB=true. So A is NOT
force-persisted by wrapToA — A is simply visible by NORMAL timing (in=-0.767,out=0.434, opacity const
1.0) and rendered on top of B, covering it. At f0 (B not yet in, in=0.033) engine==FCP (both smeared-A).
Once B enters (~f2), FCP DROPS A (fully transparent at f10) and shows B; the engine keeps A opaque over
B. A's retimeValue (frames 1→38, extrapolation=1) advances (frame 24 at f0, 29 at f10) but there's NO
hard frame-count cutoff (A is visible at frame 24 in f0). So the A-drop is the A→B CROSSFADE, not a
frame-exhaustion. THREE transitions now share this exact root cause:
  • Center: A (out=2.40)→B chain, engine held A on the tail (partly addressed by B-hold animationEndSec).
  • Light_Sweep: B fades in from the flash; engine shows B early/over-bright (timeline fix helped).
  • Smear: A(smeared) should hand off to B once B enters; engine keeps A opaque over B.
COMMON MECHANISM: FCP crossfades an OUTGOING transitionA plate to the INCOMING transitionB drop-zone
driven by their overlapping timing windows + retimeValue extrapolation — the outgoing A fades to
transparent as B takes over. The engine lacks this A→B crossfade: it composites both by static timing
windows + z-order, so the outgoing A persists opaque over the incoming B. HIGHEST-VALUE NEXT SUBSYSTEM:
decode FCP's drop-zone A→B crossfade law (how the outgoing plate's opacity ramps to 0 as the incoming
plate's window opens — likely a linear crossfade over the [B.in, A.out] overlap, or driven by the
retimeValue frame position). A single correct crossfade model would lift Center + Smear (and help
Light_Sweep). This is the shared thread behind the drop-zone-timing family. Bounded, high-ROI.

## 2026-07-26 (cont) — Smear A-drop PINNED: A hard-drops transparent at t≈0.078s (retime/media-end on 1-frame clip)
Fine-grained FCP A-plate-alone (B disabled) alpha trajectory: opaque 254 through t=0.067, then a FAST
ramp 253→239→225→205 over t=0.067-0.076, then SNAP to alpha 0 at t=0.079+. So A disappears at t≈0.078s
— FAR earlier than its raw timing out=0.434s. KEY: the "Drop Zone Transition A" CLIP has
missingDuration=0.0333s = exactly ONE frame @30fps. A's retimeValue (frames 1→38 over 1.234s,
extrapolation=1) advances the clip-frame; the 1-frame clip's media is exhausted almost immediately, so
FCP drops the plate to transparent at ~t=0.078 (retimed frame ≈3.3 scene / ≈26 clip). The engine holds A
opaque to its raw out=0.434 (ignoring that the retimed 1-frame clip ran out of media). So the fix:
a transitionA/drop-zone image with a retimeValue advancing past its CLIP's frame count (missingDuration
·fps frames) must go TRANSPARENT at that point, not persist to the raw timing `out`. The engine's
visibility uses raw [in,out]; it must additionally clip on retimed-frame > clip-frame-count. NEXT: read
the clip's frame count (missingDuration·fps) + map retimeValue frame at t, drop the plate when
frame>count. Bounded retime-subsystem fix, boundary pinned to t≈0.078. (Same retime family as the
Center/Light_Sweep A→B handoff — this is the concrete decode of the "outgoing A drops" half.)
golden 2123/0, tsc clean, working tree clean.
